# Plan 5: Groundwork's twelve chapters: implementation plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or
> superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

> **Decided (2026-10-10; recorded in DECISIONS.md by Task 1).** D116: `char` arrives in chapter 5. D117: `+` joins text and
> `String.format` formats decimals and columns, from chapter 3. D118: Python's comprehensions and `sum`/`max`/`min` become
> loops; lambdas appear only to sort; streams are only named in an appendix. D119: every chapter is accepted by automatic
> checks in three headless browsers and a panel of fresh reviewers; the author sees chapter 1, and any chapter whose
> acceptance is less certain, as a private claude.ai Artifact (replaces D19's review of every chapter). D120: one chapter at
> a time, each run as an automated workflow. D121: every push to main deploys the site. D122: the volume is `vol1`
> (https://mmmugh.github.io/groundwork/vol1/ch01-your-first-programs.html). D123: this plan is all twelve chapters (the
> roadmap's Plan 5/Plan 6 split is superseded, as D78 and D103 already said), and the porting rulings below (P-1 to P-62) are
> approved with it. D124: the front matter carries the course's byline, the one place in the course the author's name
> appears. Research: the session's reports A-D (recorded decisions; the Python volume's inventory; the machinery audit; how
> the Python course was produced), summarized in the notes worksheet's Plan 5 section.

**Goal:** the whole course live at https://mmmugh.github.io/groundwork/vol1/: front matter, twelve chapters adapted from
Python Foundations, a practice page and a quiz per chapter, worked solutions revealed one exercise at a time, the
appendices, and the downloadable code, every page proven in three browsers before it ships.

**Architecture:** three parts on `main` in ~/groundwork, committed locally and pushed only on the author's go, at six
push points (section "The six pushes"; each push deploys, D121). Part 1 (Tasks 1-10) builds the machinery earlier plans deferred to chapter 1: the decisions recorded, a
kept edit tied to its own box, the worked-solution reveal, reader links and the no-Check note, the three-way proof of
every check, a runaway-recursion box through the gates, the quiz gate, a one-chapter `--check`, the acceptance run in
three browsers, and the author's review Artifact. Part 2 (Tasks 11-22) writes the chapters, chapter 1 first as the pilot,
each through the same procedure (section "The chapter procedure"), one at a time. Part 3 (Tasks 23-25) writes the
appendices, the optional `.docx`, and closes the plan.

**Tech Stack:** JDK 25 compact source files (the pinned Temurin 25.0.4.1+1 for every gate); the build in Java
(`build/*.java`, run with the source launcher); the page in plain JavaScript modules (`web/`); Node 25.9.0 and
playwright-core 1.63.0 for the runtime gate and the browsers; the course content in the Markdown subset
`build/Markdown.java` reads.

**Spec:** ~/groundwork/DESIGN.md (sections 2, 3, 4 and 6 above all), with DECISIONS.md. The Python course
~/python_foundations/volumes/vol1-foundations/ is the source text, READ-ONLY.

## Global Constraints

- Never write anything in ~/python_foundations (read, and `git log`/`git show`, only). Never bind port 8731; tests bind
  127.0.0.1 port 0, and the review server is `node web/serve.mjs` (8740).
- Clean room (D14, D51): never open, list or search runtime/.work/build/src/, any src.zip, source.tar.gz or FdLibm.java,
  or OpenJDK/Corretto source in any form; no javap or decompiling; never a recursive grep or find over runtime/.work or
  from the repository root (search tracked files with `git grep`). Audit every subagent's transcript for forbidden paths.
- Never run the jshell binary (only runtime/jshell/test/RealJShell.java runs the real tool, with the prefs plist hashed
  before and after).
- Scratch only under build/.work/ (or the session scratchpad); never /tmp. Never symlink a fixture a test may edit.
- Every gate is proven by breaking something on purpose; the commit message names each break and its exit code.
- Commits: by path; never `--no-verify` (if the leak hook blocks, find out why); the message ends with exactly
  `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>` and never a Claude-Session line (D108). Before every push,
  `git log --format=%B origin/main..main | grep -c -E 'Claude-Session|claude\.ai/code|session_'` prints 0.
- Nothing outward without the author's go at that moment: no push, no release, no workflow run. Private review
  Artifacts for the author are authorized by D119 (the author's own request); each is private and named to the author.
- Prose (D8), in chapters, commits and comments alike: closed em dashes (word—word), American spelling ("grey"
  excepted), "course" not "book", whitelist/blacklist; "the author" for the course's author, whose first name appears only
  in the two bylines (NameTest); no pronoun for the author; home paths as ~/. The Python text's 164 spaced em dashes and
  its "behaviour" are not copied.
- Titles take a colon, `Chapter 1: Your First Programs` and `Part I: Foundations`, and an invented name takes American
  spelling ("The Quiet Harbor"): the author approved both at chapter 1's review.
- A variable "holds" a value (Python's "refers to"): the author approved it at chapter 2's review.
- Voice and pace: the Python course's, unchanged (D22); same structure, same projects and practice stories where they
  fit Java, redesigned only where DESIGN, DECISIONS or the porting rulings say (D19).
- Every box is a complete JDK 25 compact source file (`void main() { ... }`); `IO.println`/`IO.print`/`IO.readln`
  throughout; `System.out`, `Scanner`, `var`, `static`, Javadoc `/** */` and streams only in Appendix C's "What you will see
  elsewhere" (D2, D5, D6, D118, P-29).
- A stated output is never typed by hand: it is copied from the pinned JDK's run of that box, and the gates compare it.
- No box catches `StackOverflowError` or `OutOfMemoryError`, shows the null-pointer helpful message, or prints
  `Math.log` (it can differ on x86-64 in the last digit, D112); no box reads a clock; `Random` only through
  `new Random()` (it holds steady across replays, D16).
- Every exercise box has a worked solution in `_solutions.md`; every checked box has at least two right answers and one
  realistic mistake there (the three-way proof); an exercise whose answers cannot be judged fairly gets `noCheck` with
  its reason, never a check that might fail a reader who is right.

## The porting rulings (D123)

Recorded decisions cover the big choices (D2, D5, D6, D9, D10, D12, D26, D27, R1, D116-D118). The inventory found 61
further Python constructs with no recorded Java form; these rulings settle them so no writer improvises. Each names the
chapter where the Java form is first taught. When the browser runtime lacks a method a ruling names (the fork gate fails
with a compile or link error), the writer uses the closest form both runtimes run, records it in the chapter's map, and
the controller adds it here.

| P | Python | Java | First taught |
|---|---|---|---|
| P-1 | `print(a, b, c)` (commas add spaces) | `IO.println(a + " " + b + " " + c)`; arithmetic inside a join takes parentheses, `"Total: " + (3.50 + 2.25)`, and the trap without them is shown once | ch1 |
| P-2 | `print()` | `IO.println()` | ch1 |
| P-3 | `print(x, end=" ")` | `IO.print(x + " ")` | ch5 |
| P-4 | f-strings, format specs (`:.2f`, `:>7.2f`, `:<12`, `:02d`) | `+` to join (D117); `String.format("%.2f", x)`, `%d`, `%s` from ch3; widths and alignment (`%-12s`, `%7.2f`, `%02d`) where Python first aligns a table | ch3; widths ch7 |
| P-5 | `round(x)`, `round(x, n)` | `Math.round(x)` (a `long`); to n places for printing, `String.format`; to n places as a value, `Math.round(x * 10) / 10.0`. Java rounds a half up (`Math.round(2.5)` is 3, `%.2f` of 2.675 is `2.68`) where Python rounds half to even: no prose says "as in Python" about rounding, and examples avoid ties unless the difference is the point | ch3 |
| P-6 | `"-" * 30` | `"-".repeat(30)` | ch3 |
| P-7 | triple-quoted string | a text block `"""` | ch8 |
| P-8 | adjacent string literals | `+` across lines | ch8 |
| P-9 | `"5" + 1` and a string divided (TypeError lessons) | `"5" + 1` is `"51"` (a box shows it); dividing a `String` is a declared compile error | ch2 |
| P-10 | `int(x)`, `float(x)`, `str(x)` | text to a number: `Integer.parseInt`, `Double.parseDouble`; a number to text: `String.valueOf` (or `"" + x`); `int(3.9)` is the cast `(int) 3.9` (it truncates toward zero, as Python's does), `float(5)` is `5.0` or `(double) 5`, one sentence on casts; `Integer.parseInt` refuses spaces (`" 5"` throws), so text is `strip()`ped first | ch2 (`strip()` with its first use) |
| P-11 | `/` on two ints gives a float | integer division is the lesson (D5); a true division casts, `(double) total / count`, or uses a `double` | ch3 |
| P-12 | `//`, and floor division of negatives (`-17 // 5` is `-4`) | `/` on `int`s; Java truncates toward zero, so `-17 / 5` is `-3` and `-17 % 5` is `-2`: the chapter states Java's rule with that example (Python's "toward negative infinity" paragraph is replaced, not translated; `Math.floorDiv` is not taught) | ch3 |
| P-13 | `**` | `Math.pow(a, b)` (a `double`); `2 ** 3 ** 2` becomes `Math.pow(2, Math.pow(3, 2))` | ch3 |
| P-14 | `13 <= age <= 19` | `13 <= age && age <= 19`; the Python form is a declared compile error box | ch3 |
| P-15 | `"apple" < "banana"` | `"apple".compareTo("banana") < 0` | ch3 |
| P-16 | `type(x)`, `<class 'int'>` | dropped: a Java variable's type is declared (D5); the type section shows declarations and `int count = "three";` | ch2 |
| P-17 | `for x in range(n)`, `range(a, b, step)` | `for (int i = a; i < b; i++)`, counting down with `i--`; `++`/`--` taught with it; where the range reads as 1 to N (`range(1, 101)`), `i <= 100`, as Java programmers write it, and the text teaches both ends (`<` stops before, `<=` includes) (ch5) | ch5 |
| P-18 | `for x in list` | the enhanced `for (int score : scores)` over arrays and lists | ch7 |
| P-19 | `for letter in text` | `for (int i = 0; i < text.length(); i++) { char letter = text.charAt(i); ... }` (D116) | ch5 |
| P-20 | `enumerate(xs, start=1)` | an index loop, `i + 1` where numbering starts at 1 | ch7 |
| P-21 | truthiness (`if name:`, `if not xs:`) | a condition must be `true` or `false`: `if (name)` is a declared compile error box; `name.isEmpty()`, `list.isEmpty()`, `found != null` | ch4 |
| P-22 | `a if c else b` | `c ? a : b` | where Python first uses it (ch10) |
| P-23 | `x in list/str/dict/set` | `list.contains(x)`, `text.contains(s)`, `map.containsKey(k)`, `set.contains(x)`; a vowel test in ch5 and ch6 compares `char`s, `letter == 'a' \|\| letter == 'e' \|\| ...` after `toLowerCase`, and `"aeiou".indexOf(letter) >= 0` arrives with `indexOf` in ch8 | ch5 (`char ==`), ch7, ch8, ch9 |
| P-24 | `None` returned for "no answer" | `-1` for an index; `null` for an object (first printed by `map.get` in ch9, checked with `== null` from ch10; the helpful NPE message is never shown, D11); a method returning a number uses `-1` only where no real answer can be negative (ch6: `positionOf`, and the project's `getScore` when input ends); where any value can be an answer, the caller checks first (`if (b != 0)`), which is how Python's `safe_divide` box is adapted. Where an earlier method already answers "is there one?", the caller checks with it even if `-1` is spare (ch12's practice step 5, `largestFile`: `if (fileCount(root) > 0)`) | ch6 (`-1`, the caller's check), ch9, ch10, ch12 |
| P-25 | indentation makes blocks; the "BROKEN" indent box | braces make blocks; the trap becomes an `if` without braces whose second indented line always runs | ch4 |
| P-26 | default arguments | none in Java: a second method of the same name calls the first with the default (overloading, one paragraph); recursion helpers take their start values from a wrapper method | ch6, ch12 |
| P-27 | docstrings, `f.__doc__` | a `///` documentation comment (Markdown, JDK 23+) above the method; `__doc__` has no counterpart and its box is dropped | ch6 |
| P-28 | `a = double_print(5)` prints `a is None` | assigning a `void` method's result is a declared compile error, `incompatible types: void cannot be converted to int` (javac's `'void' type not allowed here` appears only for a `void` call inside an expression, such as `IO.println(say())`) | ch6 |
| P-29 | module-level data, constants, globals, `def main()` + `main()` | fields declared at the top of the compact file; a constant is a `final` field with an UPPER_CASE name (no `static`, which waits for Appendix C); `void main()` is the file's entry and calls the helper methods | ch6 |
| P-30 | scope (NameError) | a declared compile error, `cannot find symbol` | ch6 |
| P-31 | `key=lambda s: s["score"]`, `reverse=True`, `key=str.lower`, `sorted(numbers, reverse=True)` | `list.sort(Comparator.comparing(s -> s.score()))`; descending needs the lambda's type, `Comparator.comparing((Student s) -> s.score()).reversed()` (an untyped lambda before `.reversed()` does not compile); text in any case with `Comparator.comparing((String s) -> s.toLowerCase())`; numbers descending: `Collections.sort(list)` then `Collections.reverse(list)`; a map's entries by value: `List<Map.Entry<String, Integer>>` sorted with `Comparator.comparing((Map.Entry<String, Integer> e) -> e.getValue()).reversed()` (D118); chapter 9 declares that list `ArrayList<Map.Entry<String, Integer>>`, made with `new ArrayList<>(counts.entrySet())`, since `List` as a declared type is not taught. Before chapter 9, a list ordered by another list's values is built by hand: take the smallest left, repeatedly, ties kept in order (ch7's practice step 8 replaces Python's sort of `[length, title]` pairs). Two keys (Python's tuple key, `(score, name)`): sort by the second key, then by the first, since a list's `sort` is **stable** and keeps tied items in the order it found them (ch11's Try It 5 names the word); `thenComparing` stays untaught | ch7, ch9, ch11 |
| P-32 | `sorted(xs)`, `xs.sort()` returning None | `Collections.sort(list)` sorts in place; a sorted copy is `new ArrayList<>(list)` then sorted; assigning `Collections.sort`'s result is a declared compile error; `Arrays.sort(array)` for arrays | ch7 |
| P-33 | `sum`, `max`, `min`, comprehensions, generator expressions | loops (D118) | ch7 |
| P-34 | unpacking (`for k, v in d.items()`, `a, b = row.split(",")`, `a, b = b, a + b`, multiple return) | `for (String k : map.keySet())` with `map.get(k)` (an `entrySet` loop shown once in ch9); `String[] parts = row.split(","); String name = parts[0];`; a temporary variable; one value per method in ch6 (D10), a record from ch10 | ch2 (swap), ch8, ch9, ch10 |
| P-35 | `len(x)` | `array.length`, `text.length()`, `list.size()`, `map.size()`; the three spellings named where each first appears | ch5, ch7, ch9 |
| P-36 | list literals; a mixed-type list | `int[] scores = {88, 92, 79, 95};`; `new ArrayList<>(List.of(...))`; the mixed list becomes a declared compile error (an array holds one type) | ch7 |
| P-37 | negative indexes | `scores[scores.length - 1]`; a negative index is a declared `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` box | ch7 |
| P-38 | `import random`, `randint(1, 100)`, `shuffle` | `Random random = new Random();` then `random.nextInt(100) + 1`; `Collections.shuffle(list, random)`; no import (a compact file imports `java.base`) | ch5, ch11 |
| P-39 | slicing, `.copy()` of a list being sorted | `text.substring(a, b)`; arrays `Arrays.copyOfRange(a, from, to)` and a whole copy `Arrays.copyOf(a, a.length)`; lists `list.subList(a, b)`; recursion on a slice takes an index parameter instead (ch12); `[::-1]` is a loop. A Python slice past the end stops where `subList` throws, so a slice that may run past the end checks `size()` first (ch7's practice step 5) | ch7, ch8 |
| P-40 | list methods | `ArrayList`: `add`, `add(i, x)` (insert), `get`, `set`, `remove(i)` (pop; the last is `remove(list.size() - 1)`), `remove(x)` by value, `size`, `indexOf`, `contains`, `isEmpty`, `addAll` (extend); `Collections.reverse`, `Collections.frequency` (count); `.copy()` is `new ArrayList<>(list)`. On an `ArrayList<Integer>`, `remove(1)` removes index 1, not the value 1, and two `Integer`s are compared with `.equals` (`==` is false for 1000): D9's paragraph shows both | ch7 |
| P-41 | nested lists, `grid[1][2]` | two-dimensional arrays `int[][]` (D9) | ch7 |
| P-42 | string methods | `toUpperCase`, `toLowerCase`, `strip`, `replace`, `split` (`split(",")`; Python's argument-less `split()` is `strip().split("\\s+")`, explained once; `"".split(...)` gives one empty string, and trailing empty parts are dropped), `String.join`, `indexOf`, `startsWith`, `endsWith`, `Character.isLetter`, `Character.isDigit`, `Character.isLetterOrDigit`; `.isdigit()` checks of whole text are the `try`/`catch` of D26; `.title()` has no Java counterpart (the Try It that forbids it stays). `split`'s argument is a pattern, so `.` and `\|` are written `split("\\.")` and `split("\\\|")`: said once, beside `\\s+`, with a box showing `split(".")` giving `[]`; a practice story with a `\|`-separated format keeps it and reminds the reader in its step (ch8) | ch4 (strip, toLowerCase), ch8 |
| P-43 | string item assignment (TypeError) | `word.charAt(0) = 'J'` is a declared compile error; text is changed by building a new `String` | ch8 |
| P-44 | `StringBuilder` (no Python twin) | not taught; text is built with `+=`; named in Appendix C | Appendix C |
| P-45 | dict literal, `d[k]`, `d[k] = v`, `del` | `Map<String, Integer> scores = new LinkedHashMap<>();` then `put` (never `Map.of` where order is shown, D12); `get`, `remove`; the class chosen per D12's lesson | ch9 |
| P-46 | KeyError | `get` on a missing key returns `null`, and a box prints it; no exception | ch9 |
| P-47 | `d.get(k, 0)`, the counting idiom | `getOrDefault(word, 0) + 1` | ch9 |
| P-48 | `setdefault(k, [])` | `if (!map.containsKey(k)) map.put(k, new ArrayList<>());` | ch10 |
| P-49 | nested dicts, dict of lists | ch9 shows a map of maps once; from ch10, records inside lists and maps | ch9, ch10 |
| P-50 | set literal, `|`, `&`, `-` | `Set<String>` (`HashSet`, `LinkedHashSet`, `TreeSet`); a copy then `addAll`, `retainAll`, `removeAll`; `Set.of` printing is `varies` (D12) | ch9 |
| P-51 | tuples as dict keys | a record key (`record Cell(int row, int col)`): records compare by value | ch10 |
| P-52 | element types | `ArrayList<Integer>` with D9's one honest paragraph on `Integer` versus `int`; `Map<String, Integer>`. `List<T>` as a declared type is taught in ch12, for record components and the methods that read them (`size`, `isEmpty`, a for-each), holding an `ArrayList` or a list from `List.of`; a list a method grows stays `ArrayList` (P-31's ch9 note stands for ch9-11) | ch7, ch9, ch12 |
| P-53 | printed lists, dicts, `True`, `None` | Java's own text (`[a, b]`, `{Ada=88}`, `true`, `null`); an array prints as `[I@...` (an identity hash, so never a box's stated output): shown once in a ```` ```text ```` fence with a sentence, then `Arrays.toString` | ch7 |
| P-54 | `try`/`except ValueError` around `float()` | `try`/`catch (NumberFormatException e)` around `Double.parseDouble` (D26); `Integer.parseInt` reads a minus sign that `.isdigit()` refused, so wherever Python's `.isdigit()` also kept out negatives, an `if` keeps them out (the kiosk's `age < 0 || age > 120`), and the prose says the `catch` runs only when the conversion fails; a game's refusal covers the range its prompt names, `if (guess < 1 || guess > 100)`, and costs no try (ch5) | ch4, ch5 |
| P-55 | `setup` and fragment boxes | every box a complete file: the shared data is repeated in each box (D2) | all |
| P-56 | Appendix B's reference boxes | `"reference": true` boxes, with no `why` each: one sentence at Appendix B's start says they are reminders with no Run button (Task 23) | Appendix B |
| P-57 | a bare `2 + 3` / `>>>` transcript | in a box, `2 + 3;` is a declared compile error (`not a statement`); in the scratchpad, jshell answers `$1 ==> 5` (Appendix D); the appendix's jshell transcript is taken from the scratchpad (the course's front end on Ristretto, whose output a test holds to the real tool's), never from running the real tool (Task 23) | Appendix D |
| P-58 | Python's error boxes | SyntaxError: a declared compile error; NameError: `cannot find symbol`; IndexError: `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` / `IndexOutOfBoundsException`; TypeError (string or tuple item assignment): a declared compile error; KeyError: P-46; RecursionError: a declared `java.lang.StackOverflowError` box (DESIGN section 2) | per chapter |
| P-59 | method checks on lists, dicts, tuples, None, floats | cases in Java syntax: arrays `new int[] {1, 2}` for fixed collections, `List.of(...)`/`new ArrayList<>(List.of(...))` for growing ones, `Map.of(...)` (compared by value), records constructed in the case, `null` expected for "not found", `double` within 1e-9 x max(1, |expected|) | ch6 on |
| P-60 | spelling-only differences | `//` comments, `else if`, `&&`/`||`/`!`, `true`/`false`/`null`, `%`, `+=`, `while`/`break`/`continue`, escapes: written as Java. Where Python uses the word `"Python"` as example text (a word to reverse, search or count), Java uses `"Java"` (ch5, ch8) | each where Python teaches it |
| P-61 | names | camelCase for variables and methods (`countVowels`), PascalCase for records; Python's snake_case examples are renamed, and ch2's naming section teaches Java's convention | ch2 |
| P-62 | `input()` at end of input raises EOFError | `IO.readln` returns `null` when the reader ends input (Ctrl-D, End input, D16); every loop that reads stops then, `if (answer == null) break;`, said in one sentence where the first re-prompt loop appears, and a box's declared `stdin` ends with an answer that ends the loop. A box whose loop's end depends on `Random` (no fixed list can end it for every draw) declares answers every draw consumes and ends at end of input, and its sample run is a `text` fence copied from a pinned-JDK run (ch5's guessing game) | ch5 |
| P-63 | single and double quotes both make a string | a string goes in double quotes; single-quoted text is a Common mistakes row (`unclosed character literal`), and `char` waits for ch5 (D116); approved at chapter 1's review | ch1 |
| P-64 | Common mistakes rows for Python's first errors | the missing semicolon (`';' expected`), Java's commonest first error, gets its own row; approved at chapter 1's review. A Python error with no Java counterpart (`TabError`) gives its row to Java's own commonest slip at that point (`if (score >= 60);`) (ch4) | ch1, ch4 |
| P-65 | a Try It with one fixed output and no check | an `output` check wherever its verdict can be trusted, even where Python had none (DESIGN section 3), though it also passes the typed answer; approved at chapter 1's review. Where Python had only a worked solution, an input check on the same terms, when the Try It's text names the line or word the check looks for (ch4). A Try It whose data Python left to the reader takes Python's worked-solution data, in its text and starter, so its check can be trusted; a program that prints several labeled numbers and reads no input gets an input check with no typed answers, `"typed": []` (ch7). Where Python's worked-solution data cannot tell the Try It's lesson from its commonest slip, so that no check could, the starter adds the smallest piece of data that can and the Try It's text names it and the line it makes (ch10: Try It 5 adds a sixth book, Children of Dune by Frank Herbert, 1976, so grouping by author differs from one title per author) | ch1, ch4, ch7, ch10 |
| P-66 | a Try It that crashes for the reader to fix (`height / 12` on text) | a declared `compileError` box with `noCheck` (a check's starter must compile), its note saying what the fixed program prints; its worked solution is revealed and run | ch2 |
| P-67 | "do not use a built-in's name for a variable" | "start a variable's name with a lowercase letter: capitalized names belong to types" (extends P-61); approved at chapter 2's review | ch2 |
| P-68 | `float(input(...))` | `Double.parseDouble(IO.readln(...).strip())`: `strip()` before `Double.parseDouble` as before `Integer.parseInt`, so the course has one habit for typed numbers; approved at chapter 2's review | ch2 |
| P-69 | a variable first assigned in an `if`'s branches and used after it | created before the chain with no value (`String grade;`), with one sentence on block scope (a variable created inside a block exists only there) and one on javac's check that every way through gives it a value; a chain that assigns it ends in an `else`, so Python's reversed BROKEN chain gains `else { grade = "F"; }` (its lesson and output unchanged); Common mistakes rows for `cannot find symbol` after a block and `variable grade might not have been initialized` (P-30's compile-error box stays chapter 6's) | ch4 |
| P-70 | the chapter project's check | none: the project box shows the program with a sample run, because a check's starter would replace the program on the page (volumes/README.md); chapter 2's model, approved at its review | ch2 |

## The course map

The volume is `volumes/vol1/` (D122): `volume.json` is `{"number": 1, "title": "Groundwork", "subtitle": "Learn to Program
in Java", "blurb": "<the Python volume's blurb, adapted>"}`. Chapter titles follow the Python course's, except where a
recorded decision renames the subject (6, 7, 9, 10). Each chapter's practice page is `practice/chNN-<slug>-practice.md`,
its quiz `quizzes/chNN-quiz.txt`, its key `answer-keys/chNN-answers.txt` (the build links a quiz by that name, Task 4).

| Ch | Title (file) | Python source | Java it introduces (the ledger every later chapter may use) | Redesigned by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Your First Programs (`ch01-your-first-programs`) | ch01 | a compact source file and `void main()` in one honest sentence (D2); `IO.println` with text and arithmetic; `IO.println()`; joining with `+` and parentheses (P-1); `//` comments; a compile error and how to read javac's message (P-58: Python's SyntaxError box) | D2, P-1 |
| 2 | Variables and Types (`ch02-variables-and-types`) | ch02 | `int`, `double`, `String`, `boolean` declarations (D5); assignment and `count = count + 1`; camelCase (P-61); `"7" + "7"`; `IO.readln(prompt)`; `Integer.parseInt`, `Double.parseDouble`, `strip()` first, and the casts `(int) 3.9`, `(double) 5` (D6, P-10); `"5" + 1` is `"51"` and a divided `String` is a compile error (P-9); the three-line swap (D10); the first type error `int count = "three";` (D5, D123) | D5, D6, D10, P-9, P-16 |
| 3 | Expressions and Operators (`ch03-expressions-and-operators`) | ch03 | `+ - * / %`; integer division truncating toward zero (P-12), `long` and overflow (D5); `(double)` for a true division (P-11); `Math.pow` (P-13); `0.1 + 0.2`; `Math.round`, `String.format` with `%.2f`, `%d`, `%s` (D117, P-4, P-5); `+=`, `-=`, `*=`; `"-".repeat(n)` (P-6); comparisons; `==` versus `.equals` with the trap (D27); `compareTo` (P-15); `&&`, `||`, `!`; the chained-comparison compile error (P-14) | D5, D27, D117 |
| 4 | Making Decisions (`ch04-making-decisions`) | ch04 | `if`, `else`, `else if`; braces make blocks (P-25); nesting; conditions are booleans, `isEmpty()` (P-21); `strip()`, `toLowerCase()` (P-42); `try`/`catch (NumberFormatException e)` around `Integer.parseInt` and `Double.parseDouble`, the one validation tool (D26, P-54) | D26, P-21, P-25 |
| 5 | Repetition (`ch05-repetition`) | ch05 | `while`; the re-prompt loop with `try`/`catch` inside it; `for (int i = ...; ...; i++)`, `i--`, `++`/`--` (P-17); nested loops; `break`, `continue`, `while (true)`; `char`, `'a'`, `text.length()`, `text.charAt(i)` (D116, P-19); accumulators; `IO.print` (P-3); `new Random()` and `nextInt(n) + 1` (P-38); `IO.readln` giving `null` at end of input (P-62) | D116 |
| 6 | Methods (`ch06-methods`) | ch06 (Functions) | methods with parameters and a return type; `void` properly (D2); returning versus printing (P-28); early `return`; one value per method (D10); scope as a compile error (P-30); fields and `final` UPPER_CASE constants (P-29); arguments are copies; overloading as Java's answer to a default argument (P-26); `///` documentation comments (P-27); `-1` for "not found" (P-24); decomposition | D2, D10, P-26..P-30 |
| 7 | Arrays and Lists (`ch07-arrays-and-lists`) | ch07 (Lists) | arrays `int[]`, `String[]`, `{...}`, `.length`, indexing, `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException` (P-37), assignment; `Arrays.toString` (P-53); `Arrays.copyOfRange`; the enhanced `for` (P-18); two-dimensional arrays (D9, P-41); `ArrayList<Integer>` and the `Integer`/`int` paragraph (D9, P-52); `add`, `get`, `set`, `remove`, `size`, `indexOf`, `contains`, `isEmpty` (P-40); `Collections.sort`, `Arrays.sort` (P-32); loops for sum/max/min and filtering (D118, P-33); "the same object" for arrays and lists (D27); `String.format` widths (P-4) | D9, D27, D118 |
| 8 | Strings as Data (`ch08-strings-as-data`) | ch08 | `substring`, `indexOf`, `contains`, `startsWith`, `endsWith`, `toUpperCase`, `replace`, `split`, `String.join`, `Character.isLetter`, `Character.isDigit`, `Character.isLetterOrDigit` (P-42); immutability as a compile error (P-43); `split` into an array instead of unpacking (P-34); escapes and text blocks (P-7); `char` arithmetic and the `(char)` cast for the Caesar shift (`'x' + 1` is the `int` 121; `(char) ('x' + 1)` is `y`) | D116, P-42..P-44 |
| 9 | Maps and Sets (`ch09-maps-and-sets`) | ch09 (Dictionaries and Sets) | `Map<K, V>`; `HashMap`, `LinkedHashMap`, `TreeMap` and their orders, the lesson (D12); `put`, `get` (and `null` for a missing key, P-46), `remove`, `containsKey`, `getOrDefault` (P-47), `keySet`, `values`, one `entrySet` loop (P-34); lambdas to sort, `Comparator.comparing`, `.reversed()` (D118, P-31); `Set`, `HashSet`, `LinkedHashSet`, `TreeSet`, `addAll`/`retainAll`/`removeAll` (P-50); `Set.of` as `varies` (D12) | D12, D118 |
| 10 | Records (`ch10-records`) | ch10 (Tuples and Structured Records) | `record` declarations, accessors, immutability as a compile error; records compare by value, as map keys (P-51); "the same object" for records (D27); returning a record where Python returned a tuple (D10); `ArrayList<Student>` of records (D10); `null` for "not found" (P-24); grouping with a map of lists (P-48); `?:` (P-22) | D10, D27, P-22, P-48, P-51 |
| 11 | Searching and Sorting (`ch11-searching-and-sorting`) | ch11 | linear search with `-1`; binary search; selection, bubble and insertion sort on arrays with a temporary swap; `Collections.shuffle(list, random)` (P-38); a record for "index and comparisons" (P-34) | P-34, P-38 |
| 12 | Recursion and Problem Solving (`ch12-recursion-and-problem-solving`) | ch12 | recursion; `long` results (`fib` past `int`); an index parameter instead of a slice (P-39); a wrapper method with start values (P-26); memoization with a `HashMap<Integer, Long>`; a deliberate runaway recursion declared `raises java.lang.StackOverflowError` (DESIGN section 2; Task 6); the capstone over records holding lists | R1, P-26, P-39 |

Front matter (`content/00-front-matter.md`) adapts the Python course's "How to Use This Course" and names the
scratchpad. The appendices (`content/99-appendices.md`) adapt Python's A-D (D24): A, errors, compile errors as well as
runtime ones (the null-pointer helpful message explained in words, never shown, D11); B, a quick reference of
`reference` boxes; C, where next, with "What you will see elsewhere": `public class` and `public static void main(String[]
args)`, `System.out.println` and `printf`, `Scanner` with the `nextInt`/`nextLine` trap (D6), `var` (D5), `static`,
streams (D118), `StringBuilder` (P-44), Javadoc `/** */`; D, boxes and jshell, with the differences the transcript
check pins (Plan 3b's carried item) and the downloads as `.java` files.

## The chapter procedure (every chapter task follows it)

A chapter is written, gated, accepted and reviewed before it is committed; the controller runs each chapter as one
workflow (D120), and its agents follow this procedure. "N" is the chapter number, `chNN` its two digits.

1. **Input pack** (the controller assembles it under build/.work/plan5/chNN/): the Python chapter, its `_boxes.json`,
   `_checks.json` and `_solutions.md` entries, its practice page, quiz and answer key (copied read-only from
   ~/python_foundations/volumes/vol1-foundations/); this plan's Global Constraints, porting rulings, course map row and
   this procedure; `volumes/README.md`; DESIGN.md sections 2 and 3; the taught ledger (what chapters 1 to N-1 actually
   teach, from their committed files); the previous chapter's committed text as a voice sample (from chapter 2 on).
2. **The map** (writer, Opus): `build/.work/plan5/chNN/map.md` lists every Python section, box, Try It, project, practice
   step and quiz question, and its Java counterpart: ported, adapted (naming the decision or P-ruling), or dropped (with
   the reason). Nothing is silently left out.
3. **The text** (writer): `volumes/vol1/content/chNN-<slug>.md` in the Python course's structure and voice; every
   ```` ```java ```` fence a complete compact source file; snippets that are not programs are `reference` boxes or
   ```` ```text ```` fences; headings `#` to `###` only, flat lists only (build/Markdown.java's subset).
4. **Stated outputs from the JDK**: after the fences are final, run `$J build/Build.java --check --only chNN` (Task 8) and
   copy each box's actual output from the audit's report into its ```` ```output ```` fence; never type one by hand.
   Box ids are positional (`chNN-<slug>#n`): declarations are written only after the fences are final, and every key is
   re-verified after any edit that adds or removes a fence.
5. **Declarations**: `_boxes.json` (`raises`, `compileError`, `stdin`, `varies`, `reference`, `noCheck`, each with its
   `why` where required), `_checks.json` (every check with a `starter`; a method check's starter is the method's full
   signature with a body that returns a placeholder, plus a `void main()` that calls it, so Run works at once, D17),
   `_solutions.md` (a worked solution for every exercise; for every checked box two right answers in different styles and
   at least one realistic mistake).
6. **Practice page and quiz**: the practice page is the Python practice story's project in checked steps; the quiz keeps
   Python's four sections and question count, in the quiz format Task 7 defines, and its key.
7. **Gates**: `$J build/Build.java --check --only chNN` reports 0 problems; then a full `$J build/Build.java --check`
   reports 0; `node web/test/replay.mjs --site .` passes once the volume has an input box (chapter 2 on: it refuses a
   site with none); `$J build/BuildTest.java` stays 0 failed.
8. **Acceptance** (Task 9): `node web/test/acceptance.mjs chNN chromium`, `webkit`, `firefox` each report 0 failed; the
   screenshots are under build/.work/acceptance/chNN/.
9. **Review panel** (fresh agents, Sonnet, in parallel; each writes findings with file:line to
   build/.work/plan5/chNN/review-<lens>.md): (a) fidelity: the map against the Python files, nothing dropped or invented
   without a reason; (b) Java and teaching order: every construct in a box is in the ledger or introduced in this chapter's
   prose before its first use (the vocabulary gate does not hold chapter boxes), and the Java is idiomatic for a first
   course; (c) checks: each check's right answers differ in approach, its mistakes are what a learner really writes, and
   no correct answer in a reasonable style would fail; (d) prose: the Global Constraints' prose rules, the Python voice,
   no Python left in prose (`print(`, `True`, `None`, `elif`, `def`), and the chapter reads well at 390 px. An Opus triage
   classifies each finding Critical, Important or Minor, with a reason; Critical and Important are fixed (the writer
   resumes), then steps 7 and 8 run again and a scoped re-review checks only the fixes. Minor findings are fixed when
   cheap or parked in the ledger.
10. **Certainty** (D119): the controller applies Task 10's triggers (its Step 8: T1 chapter 1; T2 a section redesigned
    beyond the recorded decisions; T3 an exercise left with `noCheck`; T4 a review finding resolved by judgment; T5 a
    gate exception added or a file outside `volumes/vol1/` changed; T6 an acceptance check loosened or skipped; T7 a box
    near the page's 5 s deadline) and one more, T8: a porting ruling was added or changed for this chapter. When any
    fires, the author is shown the chapter as a private review Artifact (Task 10) and the controller waits for the
    author's approval or notes; otherwise the chapter is accepted on the evidence, and the author is told so in one line.
11. **Commit** by path: the chapter, its practice page, quiz, key and its entries in the three declaration files; the
    message lists the gates and acceptance results (with counts), the full `--check`'s `gate times:` line (Task 8), the
    panel's findings and their fixes, and the certainty triggers evaluated. The taught ledger in the SDD workspace gains the chapter's row.
12. **Push** only at the plan's push points (section "The six pushes"), each on the author's go; a chapter between them
    stays committed locally. The author may ask for a push sooner at any time.

## The six pushes

Every push deploys the whole site (D121): about 29 MB to Pages each time (the last upload was 28.5 MB, nearly all of it the
two runtimes; the chapters add a few MB in all), plus about 40 minutes of CI (the quick run, then the Pages build, whose
`--check` grows with each chapter's boxes; Task 8 watches its 30-minute limit). The git push itself is text, tens of KB a
chapter. So the plan pushes six times, each on the author's go at that moment, and never in between unless the author
asks:

| Push | When | Carries | Proven after it |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | after Task 10 and Part 1's review and fix wave | Part 1 only (readers still see the placeholder) | the quick run green on Linux, the Pages deploy green; then, on its own go, a weekly run, which is the first time Task 9's acceptance runs on Linux in three engines |
| 2 | Task 11 Step 5 | chapter 1 and the front matter | the quick run and the deploy green; chapter 1 answers at its URL and runs there |
| 3 | after Task 14 | chapters 2-4 | the quick run and the deploy green; each new chapter answers at its URL |
| 4 | after Task 18 | chapters 5-8 | the same |
| 5 | after Task 22 | chapters 9-12 | the same, and a weekly run on its own go (the Firefox job's time against its 120-minute limit) |
| 6 | Task 25 | the appendices, the `.docx` if built, the close | the same; then the course release with its site zip, on its own go |

Before each push: the D108 check prints 0, `bash ci/quick.sh node` is green on the Mac, `git log --oneline origin/main..main`
is shown to the author with the go request, and after it `git ls-remote origin` shows main at the pushed commit.

## Review Focus

- A reader who is right fails a check: a correct answer in an unforeseen style, a `double` that differs in the last bit,
  extra spaces, a `HashMap` printed in another order. Tasks 6 and 9 prove every check with two right answers and a
  mistake on both runtimes and in three browsers; the chapter panel's checks lens rejects "right" answers that differ only
  in spacing.
- A reader's kept edit appearing in another box after a published chapter gains or loses a fence (edits are kept by box
  position, `web/page/box.js:118`). Task 2 ties each kept edit to its own box's original code.
- A box that passes on the author's Mac and fails where readers run it: WebKit prints no `at` lines (D42), the x86-64 JDK's
  `Math.log` (D112), Firefox's slowness (D115). Task 9 runs every page in three engines; the weekly CI runs it on Linux;
  `Math.log` is barred from boxes (Global Constraints).
- Something used before it is taught: the vocabulary gate never holds a chapter's own boxes (it derives the vocabulary from
  them). The taught ledger and the panel's teaching-order lens hold them, and Task 7 adds quizzes to the gate.
- A quiz answer that is not what Java prints everywhere: Task 7 runs each "what does this print" question five times on the
  pinned JDK and refuses one whose output varies, and compares the key line for line.

---

## Part 1: the machinery chapter 1 needs

Run with superpowers:subagent-driven-development: a fresh implementer per task (Opus for Tasks 5, 6, 9 and 10, which
cross the build, the page and the runtimes; Sonnet for the rest), a task review after each, on `main`, committed
locally. The ledger is `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-10-plan-5/progress.md`. After Task 10, one whole-part review (Opus) and
one fix wave, then push 1 on the author's go (section "The six pushes"), then Part 2.

**How Tasks 3-10 fit together.** They were drafted against 3190510 by four drafters and are applied in order; each
quotes the exact text it changes, so where an earlier task has moved a line, the implementer finds the quoted text, and
merges (never replaces) an earlier task's edit to the same method. The names the tasks use for each other: the reveal
task is Task 3; the links task is Task 4; the proof task is Task 5; the recursion task is Task 6; the quiz task is Task 7;
the speed task (`--only` and the gate times) is Task 8; the acceptance task is Task 9; the review-page task is Task 10.
Within a task, "Task A" and "Task B" in a commit subject are replaced by the task's number. Shared files: Tasks 3, 5, 7 and
8 change `build/Vocabulary.java`; Tasks 5, 6, 7 and 8 change `build/Build.java`; Tasks 3 and 4 change `build/Pages.java`;
Tasks 5 and 8 change `build/Proofs.java`; Tasks 3, 5 and 6 change `build/ChecksTest.java`; Tasks 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 change
`volumes/README.md`; Tasks 5, 6 and 7 change the fixture volume `build/testdata/vol-fixture`, whose counts Tasks 7 and
8 assert as those tasks leave it. Task 8 lands after the proof gate exists, so it passes `only` to `Proofs.of` too and
times the proof as "proofs", where Task 5 runs it (after the fork gate). The reveal's markup is Task 3's
`<details class="solution"><summary>Show a solution</summary><pre>…</pre>…</details>`, the last child of its box, which
Task 9's acceptance drives. When a task's own text and this paragraph disagree, this paragraph wins.

**Plan 5's interfaces, which Tasks 3-10 cite by number.** (1) The volume is `volumes/vol1/` (`site/vol1/chNN-<slug>.html`).
(2) `content/_solutions.md`: `## <box id>` starts a box's section; the ```` ```java ```` fences under it, before any
level-3 heading, are its worked solutions (the first is what a reader may reveal, one exercise at a time; every one is a
right answer for the three-way proof); the fences after `### mistake` are realistic mistakes, never revealed, never
held to the starter rule, and they need not compile; a `predict` check's answers are text (its stated output is right,
```` ```text ```` fences under `## <id>` are further right predictions, ```` ```text ```` fences under `### mistake` are
wrong ones); a checked box needs two right answers and one mistake (for `predict`, the stated output counts as one right
answer); a box may have a worked solution and no check. (3) `_boxes.json` key `"noCheck": "<plain words>"` for an exercise
whose answers cannot be judged fairly: the page shows the words where Check would be, and the box has no check. (4) A method
check returning `double` or `float` compares within 1e-9 x max(1, |expected|) (finer than one `float` step, which the
course never teaches); every other type with `Objects.deepEquals`. (5) Quizzes keep the Python course's plain-ASCII format
and sections, named `quizzes/chNN-quiz.txt` with the key `answer-keys/chNN-answers.txt`. (6) The decisions D116-D124.

### Task 2: A kept edit stays with its own box

A box's edited code is kept in the reader's browser under `jf:<path>:<box id>` (`web/page/box.js:118`), and a box id is
its position on the page. Once chapters are live and a later fix adds or removes a fence above a box, a reader's kept edit
would load into a different box. The key gains a fingerprint of the code as the page was built, so an edit only ever
returns to the box whose code it edited; a box whose code changed starts fresh.

**Files:**
- Modify: `web/page/box.js:118` (and a helper above `wireBox`)
- Test: `web/test/page.mjs` (after the "Reset forgets the kept edit" check, about line 413)

**Interfaces:** Produces the key form `jf:<location.pathname>:<data-box>:<8 hex digits>`; no other file reads it.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test.** In `web/test/page.mjs`, after the check "Reset forgets the kept edit", add:

```js
  // A kept edit belongs to the code it edited: an edit kept under this box's position but for other code (an earlier
  // version of the page, or another box that stood here) never loads into it; and each box's key carries a fingerprint
  // of its own code, so two boxes with different code never share one.
  const planted = await b1.page().evaluate(() => {
    const at = `jf:${location.pathname}:ch01-run#1`;
    localStorage.setItem(at, "stale: the old key form");
    localStorage.setItem(`${at}:00000000`, "stale: another box's code");
    return [at, `${at}:00000000`].filter((k) => localStorage.getItem(k) !== null).length;
  });
  b1 = await reopen();
  check(`${engine}: a kept edit for other code at this box's place does not load into it`,
    planted === 2 && (await b1.locator("textarea.code").inputValue()).includes("Hello, <world>"), { planted });
  const b2 = b1.page().locator('.box[data-box="ch01-run#2"]');
  await b1.locator("textarea.code").fill(KEPT);
  await b2.locator("textarea.code").fill(KEPT);
  await b1.page().waitForTimeout(500); // the box keeps an edit on input; give both a moment
  const kept = await b1.page().evaluate(() => Object.keys(localStorage)
    .filter((k) => /:ch01-run#[12]:[0-9a-f]{8}$/.test(k) && localStorage.getItem(k).includes("kept")));
  const hashes = kept.map((k) => k.slice(-8));
  check(`${engine}: each box keeps its edit under a fingerprint of its own code (two boxes, two fingerprints)`,
    kept.length === 2 && hashes[0] !== hashes[1] && !hashes.includes("00000000"), kept);
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it; expected FAIL.** `node web/test/page.mjs chromium` reports
  `FAIL  chromium: a kept edit for other code at this box's place does not load into it` (the box shows
  "stale: the old key form"), exit 1.
- [ ] **Step 3: Implement.** In `web/page/box.js`, above `export function wireBox`, add:

```js
// A kept edit belongs to the code it edited: its key carries a fingerprint of the box's code as the page was built
// (FNV-1a, 32 bits), so when a later version of the course changes or moves a box, an edit never loads into another
// box's code. crypto.subtle is not used: a page over plain http has none, and this needs no secrecy.
function fingerprint(text) {
  let h = 0x811c9dc5;
  for (let i = 0; i < text.length; i++) h = Math.imul(h ^ text.charCodeAt(i), 0x01000193);
  return (h >>> 0).toString(16).padStart(8, "0");
}
```

  and change line 118 to:

```js
  const key = `jf:${location.pathname}:${box.dataset.box}:${fingerprint(original)}`; // the path carries the volume
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run; expected PASS.** `node web/test/page.mjs chromium`, then `webkit` and `firefox`: 0 failed each
  (the existing "the edited box has its code back after the crash/a reload" check still passes: an edit returns to its
  own box). Also `node web/test/runner.mjs chromium` 0 failed.
- [ ] **Step 5: Break it on purpose.** Save box.js to `build/.work/plan5/box.js.fixed`. Break 1: change the `const key =`
  line back to the key without `:${fingerprint(original)}`; `node web/test/page.mjs chromium` FAILS "a kept edit for
  other code at this box's place does not load into it" (exit 1). Break 2: make `fingerprint` return the constant
  `"deadbeef"`; the run FAILS "each box keeps its edit under a fingerprint of its own code" (exit 1). After each, restore
  with `cp build/.work/plan5/box.js.fixed web/page/box.js && cmp web/page/box.js build/.work/plan5/box.js.fixed`.

### Task 3: Worked solutions, revealed one exercise at a time

DESIGN section 6 promises "worked solutions, revealed one exercise at a time, never published together"; D24 says
per-exercise reveal only, and D37 lets a box's own data ride in that box's own markup. Today the build parses
`content/_solutions.md` (build/Vocabulary.java:128-149), holds its fences to the vocabulary gate (Vocabulary.java:59-67)
and the starter rule (build/Checks.java:198-214), and publishes none of it: build/Pages.java never reads it. This task
splits each `## <box id>` section into worked solutions and mistakes (Plan 5's interface 2), and ends each box that has
a worked solution with a native `<details class="solution"><summary>Show a solution</summary>`, as the Python course
does (~/python_foundations/build.py `exercise_html`: a details element in the box, the code in a `<pre>`, an optional
note under it; its web/app.js has no reveal code at all). The first worked solution and its note ride only in that
box's markup: never in `site/boxes.json`, the bundle or any file of every solution. A second worked solution and every
mistake are published nowhere. Practice-page steps reveal the same way, since every page's boxes render through
`Pages.appendBox` (a "project" on a practice page is a set of such steps; there is no other project box).

Decisions this task makes:
- The starter rule and the vocabulary gate keep holding worked solutions. A mistake is held to the starter rule never:
  it is a wrong answer, never revealed, and the starter left as it is, the commonest mistake there is, may well be one.
- A mistake that compiles IS held to the vocabulary gate (reported as `(mistake)`): a mistake exists to prove a check
  fails a realistic wrong answer, and one a reader at that chapter could not write (built from a later chapter's
  vocabulary) proves nothing about whether the check is fair to that reader. A mistake that does not compile is let be:
  interface 2 allows it, and javac gives no resolved tree to read its vocabulary from. Cost: one javac parse per
  mistake, as for a worked solution.
- `### mistake` is the only level-3 heading a section takes; any other is a build error, because a misspelled one
  (`### Mistakes`) would silently make its mistakes worked solutions (revealed if first, and expected to pass the check).
- The reveal is a `<details>` element: it opens and shuts with no script, so it works on every page, Java or not, and
  adds nothing to the page's scripts.

Rules for this task: scratch only under build/.work/ (never /tmp); tests bind 127.0.0.1 on port 0 (web/test/harness.mjs
already does); commit by path; never `--no-verify`; the commit ends with only the line
`Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`. Run everything from the repository root with
`J=runtime/.work/jdk25/jdk-25.0.4.1+1/Contents/Home/bin/java` (Linux: `runtime/.work/jdk25/jdk-25.0.4.1+1/bin/java`);
if `runtime/dist/fork` is absent, `source ci/lib.sh` first (it sets `JF_DIST`, ci/lib.sh:32).

    fence or heading; `mistakes` every java fence body after `### mistake`. Other fence languages are in none of them.
  - `static Map<String, Vocabulary.Section> Vocabulary.solutions(Path file) throws BuildError` (was
    `Map<String, List<String>>`): one entry per `## <id>` heading, in file order, created at the heading (so a heading
    for no such box is reported by Vocabulary.check even with no java fence). BuildError
    `<file>: <id>: "### <text>": the only level-3 heading a section takes is "### mistake"`.
  - Vocabulary.check problem line `<id> (mistake): uses what the course has not shown by chapter N: ...`.
  - Page markup, last child of `div.box`:
    `<details class="solution"><summary>Show a solution</summary><pre>ESCAPED FIRST WORKED</pre>NOTE HTML</details>`.

````java
  static void testAMistakeIsHeldToItsChapterWhenItCompilesAndLetBeWhenItDoesNot() throws Exception {
    // A mistake exists to prove a check fails a realistic wrong answer, and a reader at chapter 1 cannot write one with
    // chapter 2's Integer.parseInt: so a mistake that compiles is held to the vocabulary, and reported as a mistake, not
    // a solution. One that does not compile (the missing semicolon) is a realistic mistake too, and javac gives no
    // resolved tree to read, so it is not reported at all.
    Path p = Fixtures.project("vocab-mistakes");
    Path f = p.resolve("volumes/vol-fixture/content/_solutions.md");
    Files.writeString(f, Files.readString(f)
        + "\n### mistake\n\n```java\nvoid main() {\n    IO.println(\"Ada\")\n}\n```\n"
        + "\n```java\nvoid main() {\n    IO.println(Integer.parseInt(\"1\"));\n}\n```\n");
    Volume v = Volume.loadAll(p.resolve("volumes")).get(0);
    Map<Page, List<Box>> boxes = new LinkedHashMap<>();
    for (Page page : v.pages()) boxes.put(page, Boxes.of(v, page));
    List<String> problems = Vocabulary.check(v, boxes);
    T.check(problems.size() == 1
        && problems.get(0).startsWith("ch01-first-programs#4 (mistake): uses what the course has not shown by chapter 1: ")
        && problems.get(0).contains("member java.lang.Integer#parseInt"),
        "only the mistake that compiles is reported, as a mistake, naming what chapter 1 has not shown: " + problems);
  }
  static void testASectionTakesNoLevelThreeHeadingButMistake() throws Exception {
    // "### Mistakes" (or any other level-3 heading) would leave the fences under it worked solutions: revealed if first,
    // held to the starter rule, and expected to pass their check. Refused by name instead.
    Path f = Fixtures.project("vocab-solutions-heading").resolve("volumes/vol-fixture/content/_solutions.md");
    Files.writeString(f, "## ch01-first-programs#4\n\n### Mistakes\n\n```java\nvoid main() {}\n```\n");
    T.fails("content/_solutions.md: ch01-first-programs#4: \"### Mistakes\": the only level-3 heading a section takes is \"### mistake\"",
        () -> Vocabulary.solutions(f), "a level-3 heading other than \"### mistake\" is a BuildError naming the file, the box and the heading");
  }
````

In build/PublishTest.java, after `import java.util.List;` (:19) add `import java.util.Map;` and `import java.util.TreeMap;`,
and before the class's closing `}` (:256) add:

````java
  // DESIGN section 6 and Plan 5's interface 2: of _solutions.md, only each box's first worked solution (and its note) is
  // published, inside its own box. A second worked solution and every mistake, java or text, exist for the three-way proof
  // and reach no file of site/: no page, not boxes.json, not the bundle. Each fence carries a mark that occurs nowhere
  // else, so a mark found anywhere is the leak, and the first one's single place proves the walk reads the pages.
  static void testOnlyTheFirstWorkedSolutionReachesTheSite() throws Exception {
    Path p = Fixtures.project("publish-solutions");
    Files.writeString(p.resolve("volumes/vol-fixture/content/_solutions.md"), """
        ## ch01-first-programs#4

        ```java
        void main() {
            IO.println("Ada"); // FIRST_WORKED
        }
        ```

        ```java
        void main() { IO.println("Ada"); } // SECOND_WORKED
        ```

        ### mistake

        ```java
        void main() {
            IO.println("ada"); // MISTAKE_RUNS
        }
        ```

        ```java
        void main() {
            IO.println("Ada") // MISTAKE_DOES_NOT_COMPILE
        }
        ```

        ```text
        ada MISTAKE_PREDICTION
        ```
        """);
    var r = Fixtures.build(p);
    T.eq(0, r.exit(), "build: " + r.out());
    Path site = p.resolve("site");
    Map<String, List<String>> found = new TreeMap<>();
    try (var walk = Files.walk(site)) {
      for (Path f : walk.filter(Files::isRegularFile).toList()) {
        String text = new String(Files.readAllBytes(f), StandardCharsets.ISO_8859_1); // every byte, whatever the file holds
        for (String mark : List.of("FIRST_WORKED", "SECOND_WORKED", "MISTAKE_RUNS", "MISTAKE_DOES_NOT_COMPILE", "MISTAKE_PREDICTION"))
          if (text.contains(mark)) found.computeIfAbsent(mark, k -> new ArrayList<>()).add(site.relativize(f).toString());
      }
    }
    T.eq(Map.of("FIRST_WORKED", List.of("vol-fixture/ch01-first-programs.html")), found,
        "the first worked solution is on its own box's page and nowhere else; nothing else of _solutions.md is anywhere in site/");
  }
````

````js
      scrollW: el.scrollWidth, clientW: el.clientWidth, page: document.documentElement.scrollWidth }));

      shownCode.whiteSpace === "pre" && shownCode.scrollW > shownCode.clientW && shownCode.page <= 390, shownCode);
  }

    t.offsite.length === 0 && t.errors.length === 0, { offsite: t.offsite, errors: t.errors });
````

Replace the private `check` (:89-100) with:

```java
  private static void check(List<String> problems, String id, String kind, String source, int chapter, Vocab known) {
    Vocab used;
    try {
      used = Vocab.of(source);
    } catch (BuildError e) {
      problems.add(id + " (" + kind + "): does not compile");
      return;
    }
    beyond(problems, id, kind, used, chapter, known);
  }

  private static void beyond(List<String> problems, String id, String kind, Vocab used, int chapter, Vocab known) {
    List<String> beyond = used.beyond(known);
    if (!beyond.isEmpty())
      problems.add(id + " (" + kind + "): uses what the course has not shown by chapter " + chapter + ": " + String.join(", ", beyond));
  }
```

Replace `solutions` and its comment (:126-149) with:

```java
  /** One "## &lt;box id&gt;" section of _solutions.md. worked: the body of every java fence under the heading before
   *  "### mistake", in order; each is a right answer, and the first is the one a reader may reveal (Pages.appendBox).
   *  note: the blocks right after that first fence, up to the next fence or heading, which the reveal shows under the
   *  code. mistakes: the body of every java fence after "### mistake": realistic wrong answers, published nowhere, never
   *  held to the starter rule, and free not to compile. A fence in any other language is in none of them. */
  record Section(List<String> worked, List<Markdown.Block> note, List<String> mistakes) {}

  /** _solutions.md's "## &lt;box id&gt;" sections, by id, in file order; each made at its heading, so a heading for no
   *  such box is reported even without a fence. "### mistake" is the one level-3 heading a section takes: any other is
   *  a BuildError, since a misspelled one would leave its mistakes worked solutions. */
  static Map<String, Section> solutions(Path file) throws BuildError {
    String md;
    try {
      md = Files.readString(file, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
    } catch (IOException e) {
      throw new BuildError(file + ": " + e.getMessage());
    }
    List<Markdown.Block> blocks;
    try {
      blocks = Markdown.checkedBlocks(md);
    } catch (BuildError e) {
      throw new BuildError(file + ": " + e.getMessage());
    }
    Map<String, Section> sections = new LinkedHashMap<>();
    String id = null;
    boolean mistakes = false, note = false;
    for (Markdown.Block block : blocks) {
      if (block instanceof Markdown.Heading h) {
        note = false;
        if (h.level() == 2) {
          id = h.text().trim();
          sections.computeIfAbsent(id, k -> new Section(new ArrayList<>(), new ArrayList<>(), new ArrayList<>()));
          mistakes = false;
        } else if (h.level() == 3 && id != null) {
          if (!h.text().trim().equals("mistake"))
            throw new BuildError(file + ": " + id + ": \"### " + h.text().trim() + "\": the only level-3 heading a section takes is \"### mistake\"");
          mistakes = true;
        }
        continue;
      }
      if (id == null) continue;
      Section s = sections.get(id);
      if (block instanceof Markdown.Fence f) {
        note = false;
        if (!f.lang().equals("java")) continue;
        if (mistakes) {
          s.mistakes().add(f.body());
        } else {
          note = s.worked().isEmpty();
          s.worked().add(f.body());
        }
      } else if (note) {
        s.note().add(block);
      }
    }
    return sections;
  }
```

### Task 4: Reader links and the no-Check note

Three small reader-facing gaps (the machinery audit's risk 12 and row 18). (1) The index (build/Pages.java:301-321)
lists every page by its file slug; it should list each page by its title, in publish order, with each chapter's practice
pages under it. A page's title becomes its first `# ` heading (today `render` takes the first heading of any level), and
a page without one is a build error, since the index, the pager and the browser's tab all name the page by it. (2) A
chapter's page links its quiz (`site/<slug>/quizzes/chNN-quiz.txt`, copied by build/Publish.java:61-72) when the
volume has one for it, and its programs: `bundle.html`'s section for that chapter, which lists the chapter's `.java`
files (build/Bundle.java:46-81 writes both; each section gains `id="chNN"`). (3) `_boxes.json` gains
`"noCheck": "<plain words>"` (Plan 5 interface 3): the page shows those words where the Check button would be.

Where the links go: at the end of `<main>`, after the chapter's text and before the pager, as a short list (`{{links}}`
in web/page.html). That is the reading order (the Python course puts its quiz link at the foot of a chapter:
"somewhere to go when the reading is done", ~/python_foundations/build.py), and it is the one place with room at 390 px
(DESIGN section 5): the header (`.top`) is one row holding "Groundwork" and the volume's title, and the pager's two
links already share one flex row. A list of prose links wraps at any width; page.mjs's existing 390 px and phone
(360/390 px) checks run on both fixture pages and the index, so they cover the new list.

Decisions this task makes:
- A quiz is linked by name: `chNN-quiz.txt` with the chapter file's own digits (the rule a practice page's prefix
  follows). Any other `*-quiz.txt` (Python Foundations' `ch01-your-first-programs-quiz.txt`, or one for a chapter that
  does not exist) is a build error, not a quiz published where no page links it.
- The programs link appears only when the bundle has a section for the chapter; Pages and Bundle share one predicate
  (`Bundle.downloads`), so the link never names a section that is not there.
- `noCheck`'s type and its reference rule are checked in Decl.from (a reference box already takes no key but `why`);
  "not with a check" is checked in Checks.problems, because Decl.from reads one `_boxes.json` entry and never sees
  `_checks.json`. Its note is written right after the box's code; web/page/box.js keeps it right under the controls it
  adds, so it stays where Check would be after a Run fills the output.

Rules for this task: as Task 3's (scratch only under build/.work/, never /tmp; tests bind 127.0.0.1 on port 0; commit
by path; never `--no-verify`; the commit ends with only `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`; `$J`
as in Task 3).

````js
  // An exercise whose answers cannot be judged fairly (DESIGN section 3): no Check button, and the author's words right
  // under its controls, where Check would be, before a Run and after one.
  t = await open(engine, site, PAGES[1]);
  await ready(t);
  const nc = t.page.locator('.box[data-box="ch02-check#5"]');
  const NO_CHECK = "Any short poem is right, so there is nothing to check: run it and read it.";
  const underControls = () => nc.evaluate((b) => {
    const next = b.querySelector(":scope > .controls")?.nextElementSibling;
    return next && next.matches("p.nocheck") ? next.textContent : null;
  });
  const before = await underControls();
  await nc.getByRole("button", { name: "Run", exact: true }).click();
  await t.page.waitForFunction(() => document.querySelector('.box[data-box="ch02-check#5"]').dataset.state === "done", null, { timeout: 20000 });
  const after = await underControls();
  check(`${engine}: a box with noCheck offers no Check and says why right under its controls, before a Run and after`,
    before === NO_CHECK && after === NO_CHECK && await nc.getByRole("button", { name: "Check", exact: true }).count() === 0
      && (await nc.locator(".run").innerText()).includes("Roses are red"), { before, after });
  await t.close();

  // A chapter's page ends with its quiz, when the volume has one for it, and its programs in bundle.html; each link leads
  // somewhere real: the quiz's text, and the bundle's section for that chapter.
  for (const [p, want] of [[PAGES[0], ["quizzes/ch01-quiz.txt", "bundle.html#ch01"]], [PAGES[1], ["bundle.html#ch02"]]]) {
    t = await open(engine, site, p);
    await ready(t);
    const links = await t.page.evaluate(async () => {
      const out = [];
      for (const a of document.querySelectorAll(".chapter-links a")) {
        const res = await fetch(a.href);
        const text = await res.text();
        const id = new URL(a.href).hash.slice(1);
        out.push({ href: a.getAttribute("href"), status: res.status,
          real: id ? new DOMParser().parseFromString(text, "text/html").getElementById(id) !== null : text.length > 0 });
      }
      return out;
    });
    check(`${engine}: ${p} links ${want.join(" and ")}, and each leads somewhere real`,
      JSON.stringify(links.map((l) => l.href)) === JSON.stringify(want) && links.every((l) => l.status === 200 && l.real), links);
    check(`${engine}: ${p}: following them, nothing left the origin`, t.offsite.length === 0, t.offsite);
    await t.close();
  }
````

```java
/** A box's declaration from _boxes.json: the exemptions the author claims (it raises, it never compiles,
 *  its output varies, it is a syntax reference with nothing to run), for a runnable box its typed stdin, and, for an
 *  exercise whose answers cannot be judged fairly, noCheck: the words the page shows where Check would be (DESIGN
 *  section 3). Boxes.validate is what enforces the rules below, with Checks.problems for the one that needs
 *  _checks.json (no check beside a noCheck); this class only knows how to read one entry. */
record Decl(boolean reference, String raises, String compileError, boolean varies, List<String> stdin, String why, String noCheck) {

  /** A box with no entry in _boxes.json at all. */
  static final Decl EMPTY = new Decl(false, null, null, false, List.of(), null, null);

  private static final Set<String> KEYS = Set.of("reference", "raises", "compileError", "varies", "stdin", "why", "noCheck");
```

(c) build/Bundle.java. After `write` (:44) add:

```java
  /** The boxes of page p that get a file in the bundle: on a chapter's own page only (never practice, front matter or
   *  appendices), every box but a reference box and a box whose check has a starter (that box publishes the starter,
   *  never its own fence, which is the author's answer: the starter rule). Pages links a chapter's programs only when
   *  this is not empty, so the link never names a section bundle.html does not have. */
  static List<Box> downloads(Volume v, Page p, List<Box> boxes) {
    List<Box> runnable = new ArrayList<>();
    if (p.chapter() < 0 || p.practice()) return runnable;
    for (Box b : boxes) {
      Checks.Check c = Checks.of(v, b);
      if (!b.decl().reference() && (c == null || c.starter() == null)) runnable.add(b);
    }
    return runnable;
  }

  /** Chapter p's directory under bundle/ and its section's id in bundle.html: "ch" and its number in two digits. */
  static String chapterId(Page p) {
    return "ch" + String.format("%02d", p.chapter());
  }
```

- Replace the whole `write` method, its comment included, with:

```java
  /** Renders one volume's pages to site/<slug>/<page>.html from web/page.html and returns each page's title, for the
   *  index. site is the project's site/ directory; web/page.html is read from the web/ directory beside it. */
  static Map<Page, String> write(Volume v, Map<Page, List<Box>> boxes, Path site) throws BuildError {
    Path web = site.getParent().resolve("web");
    Path pageHtml = web.resolve("page.html");
    // No .gitattributes normalizes this on checkout: a CRLF copy must strip exactly like an LF one, not
    // fail stripLicenseHeader's regex and blame the header's shape for what is really just line endings.
    String raw = readFile(pageHtml).replace("\r\n", "\n").replace("\r", "\n");
    String template = stripLicenseHeader(raw, pageHtml);
    Path volDir = site.resolve(v.slug());
    try {
      Files.createDirectories(volDir);
    } catch (IOException e) {
      throw new BuildError(volDir + ": " + e.getMessage());
    }

    List<Page> pages = v.pages();
    // A quiz is published (Publish.copyQuizzes) to be found from its chapter's page, so each must be named for one: a
    // quiz no chapter page links would be published where no reader finds it.
    for (Path q : v.quizzes()) {
      boolean linked = false;
      for (Page p : pages) linked |= q.getFileName().toString().equals(quizName(p));
      if (!linked)
        throw new BuildError(q + ": no chapter page links this quiz; name it after its chapter's own digits, as chNN-quiz.txt"
            + " (a chapter file ch01-<anything>.md takes ch01-quiz.txt)");
    }
    // _solutions.md is read once a volume; each box's first worked solution then rides in that box alone (appendBox).
    Map<String, Vocabulary.Section> solutions = v.solutions() == null ? Map.of() : Vocabulary.solutions(v.solutions());
    List<Rendered> rendered = new ArrayList<>();
    for (Page p : pages) rendered.add(render(v, p.file(), boxes.get(p), solutions));

    Map<Page, String> titles = new LinkedHashMap<>();
    for (int i = 0; i < pages.size(); i++) {
      Page p = pages.get(i);
      Rendered r = rendered.get(i);
      titles.put(p, r.pageTitle());
      String title = escape(r.pageTitle()) + " - " + escape(v.title());
      String volume = escape(v.title());
      String part = r.part().isEmpty() ? "" : "<p class=\"part\">" + escape(r.part()) + "</p>";
      String prev = i > 0 ? link(pages.get(i - 1).slug(), rendered.get(i - 1).pageTitle()) : "";
      String next = i < pages.size() - 1 ? link(pages.get(i + 1).slug(), rendered.get(i + 1).pageTitle()) : "";
      String html = template
          .replace("{{title}}", title)
          .replace("{{volume}}", volume)
          .replace("{{part}}", part)
          .replace("{{prev}}", prev)
          .replace("{{next}}", next)
          .replace("{{links}}", links(v, p, boxes.get(p)))
          .replace("{{body}}", r.body());
      writeFile(volDir.resolve(p.slug() + ".html"), html);
    }
    return titles;
  }

  /** The end of a chapter's own page, before the pager (where the Python course puts its quiz link): its quiz, when
   *  the volume has one named for it, and its programs in bundle.html, when the bundle has a section for it
   *  (Bundle.downloads). Any other page has none. */
  private static String links(Volume v, Page p, List<Box> boxes) {
    String quiz = quizName(p);
    if (quiz == null) return "";
    StringBuilder items = new StringBuilder();
    for (Path q : v.quizzes())
      if (q.getFileName().toString().equals(quiz))
        items.append("<li><a href=\"quizzes/").append(quiz).append("\">Chapter ").append(p.chapter())
            .append(" quiz</a> (plain text: fill it in, then print it or show it)</li>");
    if (!Bundle.downloads(v, p, boxes).isEmpty())
      items.append("<li><a href=\"bundle.html#").append(Bundle.chapterId(p)).append("\">Chapter ").append(p.chapter())
          .append("'s programs as .java files</a>, to run with <code>java</code> on a computer</li>");
    return items.isEmpty() ? "" : "<ul class=\"chapter-links\">" + items + "</ul>";
  }

  /** The quiz a chapter's own page links: chNN-quiz.txt, with the chapter file's own digits (as a practice page's
   *  prefix is matched); null for any page that is not a chapter's own (the pages Bundle.downloads skips too). */
  private static String quizName(Page p) {
    if (p.chapter() < 0 || p.practice()) return null;
    return p.slug().substring(0, p.slug().indexOf('-')) + "-quiz.txt";
  }
```

```java
  private static void writeIndex(Path site, List<Volume> volumes, Map<Page, String> titles) throws BuildError {
    StringBuilder body = new StringBuilder();
    body.append("<h1>Groundwork</h1>");
    // D103: the public index must say why it is empty, never show a bare title.
    if (volumes.isEmpty()) body.append("<p>The first chapter is being written.</p>");
    for (Volume v : volumes) {
      body.append("<section class=\"volume\"><h2>").append(escape(v.title())).append("</h2>");
      if (!v.subtitle().isEmpty()) body.append("<p class=\"subtitle\">").append(escape(v.subtitle())).append("</p>");
      if (!v.blurb().isEmpty()) body.append("<p>").append(escape(v.blurb())).append("</p>");
      // Every page by its title, in publish order; a chapter's practice pages, which Volume.loadPages puts right after
      // it, are listed under it.
      body.append("<ul class=\"contents\">");
      List<Page> pages = v.pages();
      for (int i = 0; i < pages.size(); i++) {
        body.append("<li>").append(indexLink(v, pages.get(i), titles));
        if (i + 1 < pages.size() && pages.get(i + 1).practice()) {
          body.append("<ul>");
          while (i + 1 < pages.size() && pages.get(i + 1).practice())
            body.append("<li>").append(indexLink(v, pages.get(++i), titles)).append("</li>");
          body.append("</ul>");
        }
        body.append("</li>");
      }
      body.append("</ul></section>");
    }
    String html = "<!doctype html>\n<html lang=\"en\">\n<head>\n<meta charset=\"utf-8\">\n"
        + "<meta name=\"viewport\" content=\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1\">\n"
        + "<title>Groundwork</title>\n<link rel=\"stylesheet\" href=\"app.css\">\n</head>\n<body>\n<main>\n"
        + body + "\n</main>\n</body>\n</html>\n";
    writeFile(site.resolve("index.html"), html);
  }

  private static String indexLink(Volume v, Page p, Map<Page, String> titles) {
    return "<a href=\"" + v.slug() + "/" + p.slug() + ".html\">" + escape(titles.get(p)) + "</a>";
  }
```
