### Task 5: The three-way proof of every check, on both runtimes

DESIGN.md section 3: "Every check is tested three ways before it ships: a correct answer passes, a second correct
answer in another style passes, a realistic mistake fails. On both the browser runtime and the real JDK."
volumes/README.md:80-82 says this is not built. This task builds it as a gate that `java build/Build.java --check` runs
after the fork gate, the same way (a Node script under runtime/test/, started by build/ForkGate.java).

How it works, so each step below makes sense:

- **The answers** come from `content/_solutions.md`, as the reveal task parses it: a section's `worked` fences are
  right and must pass, its `mistakes` (java fences under `### mistake`) must fail. A `predict` check's answers are
  text: its stated output and each ` ```text ` fence under `## <box id>` are right, each ` ```text ` fence under
  `### mistake` is wrong. This task adds those two lists to `Vocabulary.Section` (`rightText`, `mistakeText`) in the
  reveal task's one parser, rather than parsing the file a second time.
- **The counts**: a checked box needs two right answers and one mistake (for `predict`, the stated output is one of the
  two right answers), or `--check` fails saying which is missing. This rule needs no runtime, so it holds even when the
  build's own tests pass `--no-fork-gate-for-tests`.
- **The verdict is the page's own.** `runtime/test/proofs.mjs` imports `web/page/check.js` and calls the very
  `runCheck` (method, output, input) and `predictVerdict` (predict) the page calls, with the check exactly as the box's
  `data-check` carries it (`Checks.published`). It judges each answer twice: with `NodeRunner` (the browser runtime
  under Node) and with a new `JdkRunner` in `runtime/test/jdk.mjs`, which has NodeRunner's shape (compile, run,
  compileAndRun) but compiles with the pinned JDK's `javac` and runs `java -cp <dir> Main`. So a check the page would
  judge wrong on either runtime fails the gate. A prediction is text: `predictVerdict` judges it once.
- **Doubles** (Plan 5 interface 4): the method harness compares a returned `double` or `float` within
  1e-9 × max(1, |expected|), everything else with `Objects.deepEquals` as before. The fixture's new method check proves
  it on both runtimes: `average(0.1, 0.2)`, computed either way the fixture's worked solutions compute it, is
  0.15000000000000002, one unit in the last place above 0.15.
- **Reports** name the box, the answer, the runtime and what Check said, for example:
  ```
    !! ch02-types-and-input#4: worked solution 1 fails on the browser runtime, but it is right. Check said:
      right: average(1, 4) -> 2.5
      wrong: average(0.1, 0.2) returned 0.15000000000000002, expected 0.15
  ```

**Files:**
- Create: `build/Proofs.java`, `runtime/test/proofs.mjs`, `build/ProofGateTest.java`
- Modify: `web/page/check.js:42-44` (the method harness's `jf$report`); `build/Vocabulary.java` (the reveal task's
  `Section` record, its comment, and three lines of its `solutions` loop, quoted below); `build/ForkGate.java:31-32` and
  `:50-75`; `build/Build.java:73-74` and `:133-158`; `runtime/test/jdk.mjs:19` (import) and after `:44`;
  `volumes/README.md:75`, `:80-82`, and one sentence of the reveal task's `_solutions.md` section
- Test: `web/test/check.mjs` (insert before `:222`); `build/BuildTest.java:21` (SUITES); `build/VocabularyTest.java`
  (`:63-64`, one line of the reveal task's `testAMistakeIsHeldToItsChapterWhenItCompilesAndLetBeWhenItDoesNot`, one new
  test); `build/ChecksTest.java` (one line of the reveal task's `testAMistakeMayBeWhatTheBoxPublishes`);
  `build/AuditTest.java:32`; the fixture `build/testdata/vol-fixture/content/ch02-types-and-input.md` (append a box),
  `.../content/_checks.json` (rewrite), `.../content/_solutions.md` (rewrite)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Plan 5 interface 2 as the reveal task parses it: `static Map<String, Vocabulary.Section>
  Vocabulary.solutions(Path) throws BuildError`, `Section.worked()`, `Section.mistakes()`; `Checks.of(Volume, Box)` and
  `Checks.published(Check, Box)` (what `data-check` carries); `web/page/check.js`'s exports `runCheck(runner, source,
  check, opts)` and `predictVerdict(prediction, expected)`; `NodeRunner` (`runtime/runner/node-runner.mjs`);
  `runtime/test/jdk.mjs`'s `JAVA_HOME`, `JAVA`, `JAVA_FLAGS`, `checkJdk()`; ForkGate's Node check, `exec`, `problemsFrom`.
- Produces:
  - `record Vocabulary.Section(List<String> worked, List<Markdown.Block> note, List<String> mistakes, List<String>
    rightText, List<String> mistakeText)`: `rightText`/`mistakeText` hold the body of every ` ```text ` fence before and
    after `### mistake`.
  - `static List<Object> Proofs.of(Volume v, Map<Page, List<Box>> boxes, List<String> problems) throws BuildError`
    (entries `{"id", "check", "answers": [{"label", "source", "right"}]}`; a java answer's label is `worked solution N`
    or `mistake N`, N counting that kind of fence in the section from 1; a prediction's is `the stated output`,
    `right prediction N` (N from 2: the stated output is the first right one) or `wrong prediction N` (N from 1), the
    labels Task 9's acceptance gives the same answers);
    `static int Proofs.answers(List<Object> proofs)`.
  - Build problems, one line each: `<id>: its check needs two worked solutions in _solutions.md, the second in another
    style; it has <n>`; `<id>: its check needs a realistic mistake: a java fence under "### mistake" in _solutions.md`;
    `<id>: its predict check needs a right prediction besides its stated output: a text fence under "## <id>" in
    _solutions.md`; `<id>: its predict check needs a wrong prediction: a text fence under "### mistake" in _solutions.md`.
  - `static List<String> ForkGate.runProofs(Path tools, Path proofsJson, Path dist) throws BuildError`.
  - `node runtime/test/proofs.mjs <proofs.json>` (env `JF_DIST`, `JF_JAVA_HOME`): one block per wrong verdict,
    `  !! <id>: <label> fails|passes on the browser runtime|on the JDK|as text (a prediction runs on no runtime), but it
    is right|a mistake. Check said:`, then Check's lines indented four spaces; last line `<c> checks, <v> verdicts, <w>
    wrong`; exit 0, 1 (a wrong verdict, or an empty list), 2 (usage). It writes only under `build/.work/proofs/`.
  - `export class JdkRunner` (`constructor(dir)`, `compile(source)`, `run(compiled, opts)`, `compileAndRun(source,
    opts)`) and `export const JAVAC` in `runtime/test/jdk.mjs`.
  - `--check` prints, after `<e> examples, <d> differing`: `<c> checks, <a> answers judged, <w> wrong verdict(s)`, and
    writes `<project>/build/.work/proofs.json`.
  - The generated method harness gains `boolean jf$same(Object got, Object want)`.

```js
// A method that returns double or float is compared within 1e-9 × max(1, |expected|): a reader who is right must not
// fail on the last bit. (0.1 + 0.2) / 2 is 0.15000000000000002, one unit in the last place above 0.15. Everything
// else keeps Objects.deepEquals, a double[] included.
const AVERAGE = { kind: "method", name: "average", returns: "double", params: ["double", "double"], cases: [["0.1, 0.2", "0.15"]] };
v = await runCheck(r, "double average(double a, double b) { return (a + b) / 2; }\n", AVERAGE);
check("double: a right answer that differs from the expected value in the last bit passes",
  v.passed && v.lines[0].text === "average(0.1, 0.2) -> 0.15", v);
v = await runCheck(r, "double average(double a, double b) { return a + b / 2; }\n", AVERAGE);
check("double: a wrong answer still fails, its value shown as Java prints it",
  !v.passed && v.lines[0].text === "average(0.1, 0.2) returned 0.2, expected 0.15", v.lines);
const near = (body, arg, expected) => runCheck(r, `double near(double x) { return ${body}; }\n`,
  { kind: "method", name: "near", returns: "double", params: ["double"], cases: [[arg, expected]] });
v = await near("x + 100", "1e12", "1e12");
check("double: the tolerance grows with the expected value: 100 from 1e12 is within 1e-9 × 1e12", v.passed, v);
v = await near("x + 2000", "1e12", "1e12");
check("double: ...and 2,000 from 1e12 is not", !v.passed, v);
v = await near("x + 5e-10", "0", "0");
check("double: near zero the tolerance is 1e-9: 5e-10 from 0 passes", v.passed, v);
v = await near("x + 2e-9", "0", "0");
check("double: ...and 2e-9 from 0 does not", !v.passed, v);
v = await runCheck(r, "double[] sums(double a, double b) { return new double[] { a + b }; }\n",
  { kind: "method", name: "sums", returns: "double[]", params: ["double", "double"], cases: [["0.1, 0.2", "new double[] {0.3}"]] });
check("double[]: an array keeps Objects.deepEquals, so an element off in the last bit fails", !v.passed, v);
```

````java
/*

import java.nio.file.*;
import java.util.*;

/** DESIGN section 3's three-way proof of every check (Proofs, runtime/test/proofs.mjs): every worked solution must
 *  pass and every mistake must fail, judged by the page's own web/page/check.js on the browser runtime and on the
 *  pinned JDK. */
final class ProofGateTest {
  static final Path DIST = Fixtures.DIST;
  static final Map<String, Object> IS_EVEN = Map.of("kind", "method", "name", "isEven", "returns", "boolean",
      "params", List.of("int"), "cases", List.of(List.of("4", "true"), List.of("7", "false")));
  static final String EVEN = "boolean isEven(int n) {\n    return n % 2 == 0;\n}\n";
  static final String ODD = "boolean isEven(int n) {\n    return n % 2 == 1;\n}\n";

  /** One answer, as proofs.json carries it. */
  static Map<String, Object> answer(String label, String source, boolean right) {
    Map<String, Object> a = new LinkedHashMap<>();
    a.put("label", label);
    a.put("source", source);
    a.put("right", right);
    return a;
  }
  static Map<String, Object> proof(String id, Map<String, Object> check, List<Map<String, Object>> answers) {
    Map<String, Object> p = new LinkedHashMap<>();
    p.put("id", id);
    p.put("check", check);
    p.put("answers", answers);
    return p;
  }
  /** Runs the proof gate on these proofs, with the boxes' runtime the build's tests use. */
  static List<String> prove(String name, Object... proofs) throws Exception {
    Path f = Fixtures.TOOLS.resolve("build/.work/tests/" + name + ".json");
    Files.createDirectories(f.getParent());
    Files.writeString(f, Json.write(List.of(proofs)));
    return ForkGate.runProofs(Fixtures.TOOLS, f, DIST);
  }
  /** Gives chapter 1's first box, which states "Hello, <world> & café 😀", a predict check. */
  static void predictTheFirstBox(Path p) throws Exception {
    Path checks = p.resolve("volumes/vol-fixture/content/_checks.json");
    String s = Files.readString(checks);
    T.check(s.startsWith("{\n \"ch01-first-programs#4\""), "the fixture's checks start with chapter 1's output check: " + s);
    Files.writeString(checks, s.replace("{\n \"ch01-first-programs#4\"", "{\n \"ch01-first-programs#1\": {\"kind\": \"predict\"},\n \"ch01-first-programs#4\""));
  }

  static void testTheFixtureProvesEveryCheckOnBothRuntimes() throws Exception {
    // The fixture's two checks: chapter 1's output check (two worked solutions; two mistakes, one that does not
    // compile and one that is the starter itself) and chapter 2's method check on a double (two worked solutions, each
    // one unit in the last place from 0.15 for average(0.1, 0.2), and one mistake), each answer judged on both runtimes.
    Path p = Fixtures.project("proof");
    var r = Fixtures.build(p, "--check", "--runtime", DIST.toString());
    T.eq(0, r.exit(), "full --check with the proof gate: " + r.out());
    T.check(r.out().contains("2 checks, 7 answers judged, 0 wrong verdict(s)"), r.out());
    // What the gate judged with is what the page carries: each proof's check is its box's data-check, as written.
    for (Object o : (List<?>) Json.parse(Files.readString(p.resolve("build/.work/proofs.json")))) {
      Map<?, ?> proof = (Map<?, ?>) o;
      String id = (String) proof.get("id");
      String attr = Json.compact(proof.get("check")).replace("&", "&amp;").replace("<", "&lt;").replace(">", "&gt;").replace("\"", "&quot;");
      String html = Files.readString(p.resolve("site/vol-fixture/" + id.substring(0, id.indexOf('#')) + ".html"));
      int at = html.indexOf("data-box=\"" + id + "\"");
      T.check(at >= 0 && html.substring(at, html.indexOf('>', at)).contains(" data-check=\"" + attr + "\""),
          id + "'s proof judged the check its box carries: " + attr);
    }
  }
  static void testEveryKindIsJudgedByThePagesOwnCodeOnBothRuntimes() throws Exception {
    String ask = "void main() {\n    int n = Integer.parseInt(IO.readln(\"Number: \"));\n    IO.println(%s);\n}\n";
    T.eq(List.of(), prove("proof-kinds",
        proof("m#1", IS_EVEN, List.of(
            answer("worked solution 1", EVEN, true),
            answer("worked solution 2", "boolean isEven(int n) {\n    if (n % 2 == 0) {\n        return true;\n    }\n    return false;\n}\n", true),
            answer("mistake 1", ODD, false))),
        proof("i#1", Map.of("kind", "input", "runs", List.of(Map.of("typed", List.of("5"), "numbers", List.of("10")))), List.of(
            answer("worked solution 1", ask.formatted("n * 2"), true),
            answer("worked solution 2", ask.formatted("\"Twice that is \" + (n + n)"), true),
            answer("mistake 1", ask.formatted("n + 2"), false))),
        proof("o#1", Map.of("kind", "output", "expected", "Ada\n"), List.of(
            answer("worked solution 1", "void main() {\n    IO.println(\"Ada\");\n}\n", true),
            answer("worked solution 2", "void main() {\n    String name = \"Ada\";\n    IO.println(name);\n}\n", true),
            answer("mistake 1", "void main() {\n    IO.println(\"ada\");\n}\n", false))),
        proof("p#1", Map.of("kind", "predict", "expected", "3\n5\n"), List.of(
            answer("the stated output", "3\n5\n", true),
            answer("right prediction 2", "3  \n5", true),
            answer("wrong prediction 1", "3\n4\n", false)))),
        "every worked solution passes and every mistake fails, on each runtime");
  }
  static void testAWrongVerdictNamesTheBoxTheAnswerTheRuntimeAndWhatCheckSaid() throws Exception {
    // A "worked solution" that is wrong and a "mistake" that is right: each is reported once per runtime, in the
    // order judged (each answer on the browser runtime, then on the JDK).
    var problems = prove("proof-wrong", proof("m#1", IS_EVEN, List.of(
        answer("worked solution 2", ODD, true), answer("mistake 1", EVEN, false))));
    T.eq(List.of(
        "m#1: worked solution 2 fails on the browser runtime, but it is right. Check said:\n"
            + "    wrong: isEven(4) returned false, expected true\n    wrong: isEven(7) returned true, expected false",
        "m#1: worked solution 2 fails on the JDK, but it is right. Check said:\n"
            + "    wrong: isEven(4) returned false, expected true\n    wrong: isEven(7) returned true, expected false",
        "m#1: mistake 1 passes on the browser runtime, but it is a mistake. Check said:\n"
            + "    right: isEven(4) -> true\n    right: isEven(7) -> false",
        "m#1: mistake 1 passes on the JDK, but it is a mistake. Check said:\n"
            + "    right: isEven(4) -> true\n    right: isEven(7) -> false"),
        problems, "each wrong verdict names the box, the answer, the runtime and what Check said");
  }
  static void testEachRuntimeIsJudgedApart() throws Exception {
    // The browser runtime leaves out the JDK's helpful NullPointerException message on purpose
    // (runtime/test/known-differences.json, F1-npe-catch-exception), so this program prints one line on the fork and
    // another on the JDK. As a right answer it fails on the JDK alone; as a mistake it passes on the fork alone. A
    // gate that ran one runtime and reported it for both could not give exactly these two.
    String npe = "void main() {\n    String s = null;\n    try {\n        IO.println(s.length());\n    } catch (Exception e) {\n        IO.println(\"caught: \" + e);\n    }\n}\n";
    var problems = prove("proof-apart", proof("o#1", Map.of("kind", "output", "expected", "caught: java.lang.NullPointerException\n"),
        List.of(answer("worked solution 1", npe, true), answer("mistake 1", npe, false))));
    T.eq(2, problems.size(), "one wrong verdict on each runtime: " + problems);
    T.check(problems.get(0).startsWith("o#1: worked solution 1 fails on the JDK, but it is right. Check said:\n"
        + "    Line 1 is: caught: java.lang.NullPointerException: Cannot invoke"), problems.get(0));
    T.check(problems.get(1).startsWith("o#1: mistake 1 passes on the browser runtime, but it is a mistake. Check said:\n    Correct."),
        problems.get(1));
  }
  static void testAPredictBoxsAnswersAreItsStatedOutputAndItsTextFences() throws Exception {
    Path p = Fixtures.project("proof-predict");
    predictTheFirstBox(p);
    Path solutions = p.resolve("volumes/vol-fixture/content/_solutions.md");
    Files.writeString(solutions, Files.readString(solutions) + """

        ## ch01-first-programs#1

        Trailing spaces are still right:

        ```text
        Hello, <world> & café 😀  \s
        ```

        ### mistake

        ```text
        Hello, world
        ```
        """);
    Volume v = Volume.loadAll(p.resolve("volumes")).get(0);
    Map<Page, List<Box>> boxes = new LinkedHashMap<>();
    for (Page page : v.pages()) boxes.put(page, Boxes.of(v, page));
    List<String> problems = new ArrayList<>();
    List<Object> proofs = Proofs.of(v, boxes, problems);
    T.eq(List.of(), problems, "every checked box has its answers");
    T.eq(3, proofs.size(), "three checks: " + proofs);
    Map<?, ?> first = (Map<?, ?>) proofs.get(0);
    T.eq("ch01-first-programs#1", first.get("id"), "in page order");
    T.eq(Map.of("kind", "predict", "expected", "Hello, <world> & café 😀\n"), first.get("check"), "the check its data-check carries");
    T.eq(List.of(answer("the stated output", "Hello, <world> & café 😀\n", true),
        answer("right prediction 2", "Hello, <world> & café 😀   \n", true),
        answer("wrong prediction 1", "Hello, world\n", false)), first.get("answers"), "the stated output, then each text fence");
    Path f = Fixtures.TOOLS.resolve("build/.work/tests/proof-predict.json");
    Files.writeString(f, Json.write(List.of(first)));
    T.eq(List.of(), ForkGate.runProofs(Fixtures.TOOLS, f, DIST), "each prediction is judged by the page's predictVerdict");
  }
  static void testACheckedBoxWithoutItsAnswersFailsSayingWhichIsMissing() throws Exception {
    // Chapter 1's output check keeps one worked solution and no mistake; chapter 2's method check keeps both worked
    // solutions and no mistake; chapter 1's first box gets a predict check with nothing but its stated output. The
    // rule needs no runtime, so it holds even when the build's own tests skip the gates that run under Node.
    Path p = Fixtures.project("proof-missing");
    predictTheFirstBox(p);
    Files.writeString(p.resolve("volumes/vol-fixture/content/_solutions.md"), """
        ## ch01-first-programs#4

        ```java
        void main() {
            IO.println("Ada");
        }
        ```

        ## ch02-types-and-input#4

        ```java
        double average(double a, double b) {
            return (a + b) / 2;
        }

        void main() {
            IO.println(average(2, 3));
        }
        ```

        ```java
        double average(double a, double b) {
            return a / 2 + b / 2;
        }

        void main() {
            IO.println(average(1, 4));
        }
        ```
        """);
    var r = Fixtures.build(p, "--check", "--no-fork-gate-for-tests");
    T.eq(1, r.exit(), "exit: " + r.out());
    for (String line : List.of(
        "ch01-first-programs#1: its predict check needs a right prediction besides its stated output: a text fence under \"## ch01-first-programs#1\" in _solutions.md",
        "ch01-first-programs#1: its predict check needs a wrong prediction: a text fence under \"### mistake\" in _solutions.md",
        "ch01-first-programs#4: its check needs two worked solutions in _solutions.md, the second in another style; it has 1",
        "ch01-first-programs#4: its check needs a realistic mistake: a java fence under \"### mistake\" in _solutions.md",
        "ch02-types-and-input#4: its check needs a realistic mistake: a java fence under \"### mistake\" in _solutions.md"))
      T.check(r.out().contains("  !! " + line + "\n"), "says what is missing: " + line + " in: " + r.out());
    T.check(r.out().contains(", 5 problem(s)"), "and nothing else: " + r.out());
    T.check(!Files.exists(p.resolve("build/.work/proofs.json")) && !r.out().contains("answers judged"),
        "the proof itself runs only with the gates that run under Node: " + r.out());
  }
  static void testAnEmptyProofListIsAFailure() throws Exception {
    T.check(!prove("proof-empty").isEmpty(), "zero proofs is not a pass (Build.java never sends an empty list)");
  }
}
````

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

3a. `web/page/check.js`, in `helpers` (lines 42-44). Replace:

```js
  "void jf$report(int i, Object got, Object want) {",
  '  jf$say(i + (java.util.Objects.deepEquals(got, want) ? " ok" : " fail " + jf$show(got)));',
  "}",
```

with:

```js
  "void jf$report(int i, Object got, Object want) {",
  '  jf$say(i + (jf$same(got, want) ? " ok" : " fail " + jf$show(got)));',
  "}",
  // A double or float is right within 1e-9 × max(1, |expected|), so a reader who is right never fails on the last bit:
  // (0.1 + 0.2) / 2 is 0.15000000000000002, one unit in the last place above 0.15. Anything else, a double[] included,
  // is compared as before.
  "boolean jf$same(Object got, Object want) {",
  "  if ((got instanceof Double || got instanceof Float) && (want instanceof Double || want instanceof Float)) {",
  "    double g = ((Number) got).doubleValue(), w = ((Number) want).doubleValue();",
  "    return Double.compare(g, w) == 0 || Math.abs(g - w) <= 1e-9 * Math.max(1, Math.abs(w));",
  "  }",
  "  return java.util.Objects.deepEquals(got, want);",
  "}",
```

The helpers follow the generated `main`, so no line of the reader's file or of a case call moves (explainCompile's
columns and `harness.calls` are unchanged). `Double.compare` keeps NaN equal to NaN and an infinity equal to itself, as
`deepEquals` did.

3c. `runtime/test/jdk.mjs`. Change line 19 from `import { spawnSync } from "node:child_process";` to
`import { spawn, spawnSync } from "node:child_process";`, add `export const JAVAC = path.join(JAVA_HOME, "bin", "javac");`
after line 22 (`export const JAVA = ...`), and append after line 44 (the end of `runJdk`):

```js

// The environment runJdk gives a run (and build/Jdk.java's cleanEnvironment the audit's): nothing of the caller's.
const ENV = { PATH: "/usr/bin:/bin", LANG: "en_US.UTF-8", HOME: process.env.HOME };

// javac's report on the command line, as the diagnostics web/page/check.js reads from the browser runtime's javac: one
// per "Main.java:<line>: error: <message>" line, its column from the caret under the echoed source line, and the
// indented lines after the caret ("symbol: ...", "location: ...") joined to the message, where javax.tools puts them.
function javacDiagnostics(stderr) {
  const lines = stderr.split("\n"), out = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
    const m = /^Main\.java:(\d+): error: (.*)$/.exec(lines[i]);
    if (!m) continue;
    const d = { phase: "javac", line: Number(m[1]), column: null, message: m[2] };
    const caret = (lines[i + 2] ?? "").indexOf("^");
    if (caret >= 0) {
      d.column = caret + 1;
      for (let j = i + 3; j < lines.length && /^\s/.test(lines[j]); j++) d.message += "\n" + lines[j];
    }
    out.push(d);
  }
  return out;
}

// The runner web/page/check.js's runCheck drives (compile, run and compileAndRun, as NodeRunner has them), on the pinned
// JDK, for the three-way proof of every check (runtime/test/proofs.mjs). javac compiles Main.java into a directory of its
// own; each run is a fresh `java -cp <dir> Main` with its stdin, killed at the deadline or the page's output caps. A
// result has NodeRunner's shape, so the page's code reads both alike: an uncaught exception ends a run "ok" with the
// JDK's trace on stderr, and System.exit(N), N not 0, is "exit-N" with exitCode N. Unlike NodeRunner it cannot tell
// System.exit(0) from an ordinary end: both are "ok" with exitCode null, so a method that calls System.exit(0) still
// fails its check, in other words ("the program ended unexpectedly").
export class JdkRunner {
  constructor(dir) {
    this.dir = dir;
    this.n = 0;
    fs.rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
  }
  async compile(source) {
    const dir = path.join(this.dir, String(++this.n));
    fs.mkdirSync(path.join(dir, "classes"), { recursive: true });
    fs.writeFileSync(path.join(dir, "Main.java"), source);
    const p = spawnSync(JAVAC, ["--release", "25", "-encoding", "UTF-8", "-d", "classes", "Main.java"],
      { cwd: dir, encoding: "utf8", timeout: 60000, env: ENV });
    if (p.status !== 0) return { ok: false, stage: "javac", diagnostics: javacDiagnostics(p.stderr ?? "") };
    // runCheck copies what compile gave as "wasm" with slice() before each input run (NodeRunner hands the bytes to a
    // worker); the classes' directory needs no copy.
    return { ok: true, wasm: { classes: path.join(dir, "classes"), slice() { return this; } }, diagnostics: [] };
  }
  run(compiled, { stdin = "", deadlineMs = 5000, outputLimitChars = Infinity, outputLimitLines = Infinity } = {}) {
    return new Promise((resolve) => {
      const p = spawn(JAVA, [...JAVA_FLAGS, "-cp", compiled.classes, "Main"], { env: ENV });
      let stdout = "", stderr = "", lines = 0, stopped = null;
      const stop = (why) => { if (!stopped) { stopped = why; p.kill("SIGKILL"); } };
      const timer = setTimeout(() => stop("timeout"), deadlineMs);
      p.stdout.setEncoding("utf8");
      p.stderr.setEncoding("utf8");
      p.stdout.on("data", (s) => {
        stdout += s;
        lines += s.split("\n").length - 1;
        if (stdout.length > outputLimitChars || lines > outputLimitLines) stop("output-limit");
      });
      p.stderr.on("data", (s) => { stderr += s; });
      p.stdin.on("error", () => {}); // a program that ends without reading its input closes stdin under us
      p.stdin.end(stdin);
      p.on("close", (code) => {
        clearTimeout(timer);
        const uncaught = code === 1 && /Exception in thread "/.test(stderr);
        const status = stopped ?? (code === 0 || uncaught ? "ok" : code === null ? "crashed" : `exit-${code}`);
        resolve({ status, exception: null, exitCode: /^exit-/.test(status) ? code : null, stdout, stderr });
      });
    });
  }
  async compileAndRun(source, opts = {}) {
    const c = await this.compile(source);
    if (!c.ok) return { compile: c, status: "compile-error", stdout: "", stderr: "" };
    return { compile: { ok: true }, ...(await this.run(c.wasm, opts)) };
  }
}
```

3d. Create `runtime/test/proofs.mjs`:

```js
/*
 *  Copyright 2026 Groundwork contributors.
 *
 *  Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
 *  you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
 *  You may obtain a copy of the License at
 *
 *       http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
 *
 *  Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
 *  distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
 *  WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
 *  See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
 *  limitations under the License.
 */
// The three-way proof of every check (DESIGN section 3): for each checked box Build.java --check writes to its proofs
// file, every worked solution must pass and every mistake must fail, judged by the page's own web/page/check.js
// (runCheck) on the browser runtime and on the pinned JDK, so a check the page would get wrong cannot pass. A
// prediction is text: the page's predictVerdict judges it once, and no runtime runs it.
//   node runtime/test/proofs.mjs <proofs.json>    (JF_DIST: the runtime to test; JF_JAVA_HOME: the JDK)
// proofs.json, from build/Proofs.java: [{"id": "<box id>", "check": <its data-check>, "answers": [{"label", "source", "right"}]}]
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { NodeRunner } from "../runner/node-runner.mjs";
import { checkJdk, JdkRunner } from "./jdk.mjs";
import { runCheck, predictVerdict } from "../../web/page/check.js";

const RUNTIME = path.resolve(fileURLToPath(new URL("..", import.meta.url)));
const file = process.argv[2];
if (!file) { console.log("usage: node runtime/test/proofs.mjs <proofs.json>"); process.exit(2); }
const proofs = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8"));
if (!Array.isArray(proofs) || proofs.length === 0) { console.log("no checks to prove"); process.exit(1); }
checkJdk();
const fork = new NodeRunner(process.env.JF_DIST || path.join(RUNTIME, "dist", "fork"));
const runtimes = [["on the browser runtime", fork], ["on the JDK", new JdkRunner(path.join(RUNTIME, "..", "build", ".work", "proofs"))]];

// What Check said, in the page's own words, a line each: every case or run, why it stopped, or why the file did not
// compile.
function said(v) {
  if (v.compile) return v.compile.kind === "signature" ? [v.compile.message]
    : ["it does not compile:", ...v.compile.diagnostics.map((d) => `${d.line != null ? `line ${d.line}: ` : ""}${String(d.message).split("\n")[0]}`)];
  const lines = (v.lines ?? []).map((l) => `${l.ok ? "right" : "wrong"}: ${l.text}`);
  if (v.stopped) lines.push(v.stopped);
  if (v.text) lines.push(v.text);
  return lines.join("\n").split("\n");
}

let verdicts = 0, wrong = 0;
for (const p of proofs) {
  for (const a of p.answers) {
    const judged = [];
    if (p.check.kind === "predict") judged.push(["as text (a prediction runs on no runtime)", predictVerdict(a.source, p.check.expected)]);
    else for (const [where, runner] of runtimes) judged.push([where, await runCheck(runner, a.source, p.check)]);
    for (const [where, v] of judged) {
      verdicts++;
      if (v.passed === a.right) continue;
      wrong++;
      console.log(`  !! ${p.id}: ${a.label} ${a.right ? "fails" : "passes"} ${where}, but it is ${a.right ? "right" : "a mistake"}. Check said:\n`
        + said(v).map((l) => "    " + l).join("\n"));
    }
  }
}
await fork.close();
console.log(`${proofs.length} checks, ${verdicts} verdicts, ${wrong} wrong`);
process.exit(wrong ? 1 : 0);
```

3e. `build/ForkGate.java`. Replace the class comment (lines 31-32) with:

```java
/** D-P2-1: holds the browser runtime to what the pinned JDK does on every course example, by running
 *  runtime/test/examples.mjs under Node; and proves every check three ways on both (runtime/test/proofs.mjs), the
 *  same way. --check fails (never skips) without Node 25 or later. */
```

and replace `run` (lines 50-75) with:

```java
  /** Runs runtime/test/examples.mjs (found under tools) on examplesJson, testing dist against the pinned
   *  JDK this JVM is running (java.home). Returns each "  !! " problem block from its output, or, when it
   *  exited non-zero with none (Node itself failed to start, or the script's own usage/empty-list message),
   *  the tail of what it printed, so a fork-gate failure is never silently swallowed. */
  static List<String> run(Path tools, Path examplesJson, Path dist) throws BuildError {
    return runScript(tools, "runtime/test/examples.mjs", examplesJson, dist, "the fork gate");
  }

  /** Runs runtime/test/proofs.mjs, the three-way proof of every check (DESIGN section 3), on proofsJson (Proofs.of's
   *  entries), the same way: every answer is judged by the page's own check.js on dist and on the pinned JDK this JVM
   *  is running. Returns each wrong verdict as a problem, as run does a disagreement. */
  static List<String> runProofs(Path tools, Path proofsJson, Path dist) throws BuildError {
    return runScript(tools, "runtime/test/proofs.mjs", proofsJson, dist, "the proof gate");
  }

  private static List<String> runScript(Path tools, String script, Path json, Path dist, String gate) throws BuildError {
    String versionOutput;
    try {
      versionOutput = exec(new ProcessBuilder("node", "--version"), tools, Duration.ofSeconds(10)).output();
    } catch (IOException e) {
      return List.of(NEEDS_NODE + "; node was not found");
    }
    String badVersion = checkNodeVersion(versionOutput);
    if (badVersion != null) return List.of(badVersion);

    ProcessBuilder pb = new ProcessBuilder("node", script, json.toString());
    pb.environment().put("JF_DIST", dist.toString());
    pb.environment().put("JF_JAVA_HOME", System.getProperty("java.home"));
    Result r;
    try {
      r = exec(pb, tools, DEADLINE);
    } catch (IOException e) {
      throw new BuildError("running " + gate + ": " + e.getMessage());
    }
    if (r.timedOut()) return List.of(gate + " did not finish within 30 minutes");
    return problemsFrom(r.output(), r.exitCode());
  }
```

3f. Create `build/Proofs.java`:

```java
/*
 *  Copyright 2026 Groundwork contributors.
 *
 *  Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
 *  you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
 *  You may obtain a copy of the License at
 *
 *       http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
 *
 *  Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
 *  distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
 *  WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
 *  See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
 *  limitations under the License.
 */
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.LinkedHashMap;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;

/** DESIGN section 3: every check is tested three ways before it ships. For each checked box, the answers its
 *  _solutions.md section gives (Vocabulary.solutions): every worked solution is right and must pass, every java fence
 *  under "### mistake" is a realistic mistake and must fail. runtime/test/proofs.mjs judges each one with the page's
 *  own web/page/check.js on the browser runtime and on the pinned JDK (ForkGate.runProofs). A checked box needs two
 *  worked solutions, the second in another style, and a mistake. A predict box's answers are text: its stated output
 *  is the first right one, and it needs one more (a text fence under its heading) and a wrong one (a text fence under
 *  "### mistake"). */
final class Proofs {
  private Proofs() {}

  /** A box with no section in _solutions.md. */
  private static final Vocabulary.Section NONE = new Vocabulary.Section(List.of(), List.of(), List.of(), List.of(), List.of());

  /** v's checked boxes, in page order, as proofs.json entries {id, check, answers: [{label, source, right}]}, check
   *  being exactly what the box's data-check carries (Checks.published). Each checked box without the answers its
   *  proof needs adds a problem saying which are missing. */
  static List<Object> of(Volume v, Map<Page, List<Box>> boxes, List<String> problems) throws BuildError {
    Map<String, Vocabulary.Section> sections = v.solutions() == null ? Map.of() : Vocabulary.solutions(v.solutions());
    List<Object> proofs = new ArrayList<>();
    for (List<Box> list : boxes.values()) {
      for (Box b : list) {
        Checks.Check c = Checks.of(v, b);
        if (c == null) continue;
        Vocabulary.Section s = sections.getOrDefault(b.id(), NONE);
        List<Object> answers = new ArrayList<>();
        if (c.kind().equals("predict")) {
          answers.add(answer("the stated output", b.expected(), true));
          // The stated output is the first right prediction, so the text fences under the heading count from 2.
          add(answers, "right prediction ", 2, s.rightText(), true);
          add(answers, "wrong prediction ", 1, s.mistakeText(), false);
          if (s.rightText().isEmpty())
            problems.add(b.id() + ": its predict check needs a right prediction besides its stated output: a text fence under \"## "
                + b.id() + "\" in _solutions.md");
          if (s.mistakeText().isEmpty())
            problems.add(b.id() + ": its predict check needs a wrong prediction: a text fence under \"### mistake\" in _solutions.md");
        } else {
          add(answers, "worked solution ", 1, s.worked(), true);
          add(answers, "mistake ", 1, s.mistakes(), false);
          if (s.worked().size() < 2)
            problems.add(b.id() + ": its check needs two worked solutions in _solutions.md, the second in another style; it has "
                + s.worked().size());
          if (s.mistakes().isEmpty())
            problems.add(b.id() + ": its check needs a realistic mistake: a java fence under \"### mistake\" in _solutions.md");
        }
        Map<String, Object> proof = new LinkedHashMap<>();
        proof.put("id", b.id());
        proof.put("check", Checks.published(c, b));
        proof.put("answers", answers);
        proofs.add(proof);
      }
    }
    return proofs;
  }

  /** How many answers proofs hold in all. */
  static int answers(List<Object> proofs) {
    int n = 0;
    for (Object p : proofs) n += ((List<?>) ((Map<?, ?>) p).get("answers")).size();
    return n;
  }

  /** Each of sources as an answer, labeled label and its number, counting from first. */
  private static void add(List<Object> answers, String label, int first, List<String> sources, boolean right) {
    for (int i = 0; i < sources.size(); i++) answers.add(answer(label + (first + i), sources.get(i), right));
  }

  private static Map<String, Object> answer(String label, String source, boolean right) {
    Map<String, Object> a = new LinkedHashMap<>();
    a.put("label", label);
    a.put("source", source);
    a.put("right", right);
    return a;
  }
}
```

### Task 6: A runaway recursion box through the gates (chapter 12)

DESIGN.md:118: "Chapter 12's deliberate runaway recursion is declared as an example that crashes on purpose." Today such
a box cannot pass `--check`. The browser runtime reports a stack overflow with the JDK's first line alone,
`Exception in thread "main" java.lang.StackOverflowError` (runtime/runner/java-line.js:23-49: a WebAssembly engine's
overflow is no Java exception, so no frame is known), while the JDK prints up to 1,024 `at Main...` frames below it.
Audit's stated output keeps the program's own frames (build/Audit.java:181-189), so the box would have to state about
1,024 lines the page never shows; and the fork gate compares the program's own frames (runtime/test/compare.mjs:21-27,
runtime/test/examples.mjs:51-53), so the box disagrees whatever it states. The browser side is already proven: the
page's own fixture runs the same program (web/test/vol-page ch01-run#5) in three engines (web/test/page.mjs:157-160).

This task makes a box declared `"raises": "java.lang.StackOverflowError"` state just the first line, and leaves its
frames out of the audit's comparisons and the fork gate's, keyed to that declaration. Everything else is still compared:
the status, stdout and that first line. So a box that does not overflow, throws something else first, prints something
it does not state, states a frame, or catches the overflow (the JDK can, the browser runtime cannot, and DESIGN section 2
says the differential gate enforces that no example tries) still fails. An overflow no box declares is compared frame by
frame, as before. The fixture gains such a box, so ForkGateTest and AuditTest prove it end to end through `--check`.

`OutOfMemoryError` needs nothing here. DESIGN section 2 declares only chapter 12's recursion as crashing on purpose, says
no example tries to catch either error, and the Python course has no `MemoryError` example (a search of
~/python_foundations/volumes/vol1-foundations finds `RecursionError` in chapter 12 and the appendices, and no
`MemoryError`). No box will declare `raises` OutOfMemoryError, so no exemption is added. One that did would still be
compared frame by frame, and how soon the JDK runs out depends on the machine's memory; that was read from the code and
was not run.

  /** DESIGN section 2: chapter 12's runaway recursion crashes on purpose, declared raises java.lang.StackOverflowError.

```java
  static final String DOWN = "int down(int n) {\n    return down(n + 1) + 1;\n}\n\nvoid main() {\n    IO.println(down(0));\n}\n";
  /** One example as Build.java writes it, raises included (null when the box declares none). */
  static Path example(String name, String src, String raises) throws Exception {
    Map<String, Object> e = new LinkedHashMap<>();
    e.put("id", "s#1");
    e.put("src", src);
    e.put("stdin", "");
    e.put("varies", false);
    e.put("raises", raises);
    return examples(name, Json.write(List.of(e)));
  }
  /** DESIGN section 2: chapter 12's runaway recursion crashes on purpose. The fork reports the overflow with the JDK's
   *  first line alone (runtime/runner/java-line.js); the JDK prints up to 1,024 frames below it. Declared, the box is
   *  compared without frames; undeclared, frame by frame as before; and a program that catches the overflow, which
   *  the JDK can and the fork cannot, disagrees either way (DESIGN section 2: the differential gate enforces that no
   *  example tries). */
  static void testADeclaredRunawayRecursionIsComparedWithoutItsFrames() throws Exception {
    String overflow = "java.lang.StackOverflowError";
    T.eq(List.of(), ForkGate.run(Fixtures.TOOLS, example("fork-overflow", DOWN, overflow), DIST),
        "declared, it agrees: the same status, stdout and first line");
    var undeclared = ForkGate.run(Fixtures.TOOLS, example("fork-overflow-undeclared", DOWN, null), DIST);
    T.check(undeclared.size() == 1 && undeclared.get(0).startsWith("s#1: the browser runtime and the JDK disagree")
        && undeclared.get(0).contains("Main.down(Main.java:2)"),
        "undeclared, the JDK's frames are compared, and the fork has none: " + undeclared);
    String caught = DOWN.replace("    IO.println(down(0));",
        "    try {\n        IO.println(down(0));\n    } catch (StackOverflowError e) {\n        IO.println(\"caught\");\n    }");
    var caughtProblems = ForkGate.run(Fixtures.TOOLS, example("fork-overflow-caught", caught, overflow), DIST);
    T.check(caughtProblems.size() == 1 && caughtProblems.get(0).startsWith("s#1: the browser runtime and the JDK disagree"),
        "a program that catches the overflow still disagrees: " + caughtProblems);
  }
```

After `stated` (it ends at line 189), add:

```java

  /** run, but for a box declared to raise java.lang.StackOverflowError (DESIGN section 2: chapter 12's runaway
   *  recursion crashes on purpose) that ended uncaught, with stderr cut to its first line. The browser runtime reports
   *  a stack overflow with the JDK's first line alone (runtime/runner/java-line.js: the engine's overflow is no Java
   *  exception, so no frame is known), where the JDK prints up to 1,024 frames below it. So such a box states that
   *  line alone, and its frames are compared neither with the stated output nor between the audit's two runs. Its
   *  status, stdout and first line still are: a box that does not overflow, throws something else or prints something
   *  else still fails. */
  static Jdk.Run withoutOverflowFrames(Decl decl, Jdk.Run run) {
    if (!"java.lang.StackOverflowError".equals(decl.raises()) || !run.status().equals("uncaught")) return run;
    int end = run.stderr().indexOf('\n');
    return new Jdk.Run(run.status(), run.stdout(), end < 0 ? run.stderr() : run.stderr().substring(0, end + 1));
  }
```

The raises rule (`run.stderr().startsWith("Exception in thread \"main\" " + decl.raises())`) reads the first line, so it
is unchanged; a run that wrote to System.err first is `exit-1`, not `uncaught`, and is still refused (D73).

### Task 7: Quizzes: the format, the "What does this print?" gate, and quiz vocabulary

DESIGN section 6 promises "a quiz per chapter, plain text, every 'what does this print' answer verified on the real JDK,
answer keys never published", and section 4 holds "a worked solution, practice step, check starter or quiz" to the
vocabulary the chapters have taught. Today a quiz is only copied (build/Publish.java:61-72) and a key only guarded
(Publish.java:102): nothing reads either (C-machinery rows 12 and 21), and the fixture's quiz (`1. What does
IO.println("Hi") print?`) is in no format the build could read. This task fixes the format, in the Python course's layout
(~/python_foundations/volumes/vol1-foundations/quizzes/, answer-keys/, scripts/make_quizzes.py: read-only), and adds the
gate.

**The format, decided here** (volumes/README.md gains it in Step 3):
- `quizzes/chNN-quiz.txt` is chapter NN's quiz (published as it is); `answer-keys/chNN-answers.txt` is its key (never
  published). NN is the digit string the chapter's own file uses. Every quiz needs its key, every key its quiz, and a
  quiz needs a chapter NN in the volume.
- The Python layout: `GROUNDWORK`, `Chapter N - <title>`, then the quiz's `Name:`/`Date:` line or the key's line
  `ANSWER KEY`; sections `VOCABULARY`, `READING CODE`, `WRITING CODE`, `WHY IT WORKS`, each underlined with hyphens; a
  question starts at the margin with its number right-aligned in two columns (` 5. `, `12. `), further lines indented 4,
  wrapped at 74; in the key, each blank's answer on its own line `      ANSWER: <answer>` (6 spaces). A quiz is plain ASCII
  (a `.txt` cannot say its encoding; in the Python course a phone showed an em dash as "a-EUR-"). A quiz holds no `ANSWER:`
  line, and a key always holds `ANSWER KEY` (the words `Publish.guard` refuses to publish).
- **Finding the code.** A question whose line is exactly ` N. What does this print?` is run. Its code is the lines under
  it that are blank or indented 8 spaces, from the first indented one, with 8 spaces removed; a blank line inside is kept,
  blank lines at its end are dropped. In the quiz it is followed by the place to write (4 spaces, 36 underscores); in the
  key by its `ANSWER:` line (6 spaces). Both are indented fewer than 8, so the code's end is never in doubt. A question
  that says "does this print" in any other words is reported, never skipped.
- **Running it.** If a line of the code starts `void main(` at the code's own margin, the code is a whole compact source
  file and runs as written; otherwise it is statements, run as `main`'s body (`void main() {`, each non-blank line
  indented 4, `}`). Code that needs a method or an import beside `main` is written whole. It compiles on the pinned JDK
  (build/Jdk.java), runs five times with no input, and must run to its end (`ok`), print the same every time, print no
  identity hash (`[I@1b6d3586`), and print exactly
  its key's answer.
- **The answer.** The key repeats the question and its code exactly (the gate requires the same code) and has one
  `ANSWER:` line for it: the rest of that line after `ANSWER: ` (one space; any further leading spaces are the program's
  own) is the output's first line; each further output line follows, indented 10 spaces (the Python keys' indent), with
  those 10 removed; a blank output line is a blank line (with or without its 10 spaces); the answer ends at the first line
  that is neither blank nor indented 10. Answer and output are compared after `Audit.tidy` (build/Audit.java:206-213), the
  stated-output tidying: CRLF as LF, trailing spaces and blank lines at either end ignored, anything else a difference.
- **Vocabulary.** The code, as it runs (wrapped), is held to the chapter's vocabulary: a quiz for chapter N may use what
  chapters 1 to N teach (Vocabulary's own `knownByChapter`, build/Vocabulary.java:107-124). Every other question (a term,
  code with a blank in it, a reason) is neither run nor vocabulary-checked; the review reads it.

**The fixture** changes so the gate has something real to prove: chapter 1's quiz gets two "What does this print?"
questions, a statement snippet whose output has a blank line in it (`IO.println();` between two lines) and a whole program
with its own `void main` whose code has a blank line in it, plus one vocabulary question that is not run; its key gets
the `ANSWER KEY` line and the answers (the interior blank output line written as an empty line). Everything they use is
taught by the fixture's chapter 1 (`IO.println`, `int[]`, `new int[3]`, `a[0]`: ch01-first-programs#1 and #2). The quiz
stays the only file in quizzes/, so VolumeTest:30 (`1` quiz) and PagesTest:134 hold.

```java
/*

import java.nio.file.*;
import java.util.*;

/** DESIGN section 6's "every 'what does this print' answer verified on the real JDK", on the fixture's chapter 1 quiz (a
 *  statement snippet whose output has a blank line, a whole program whose code has one) and its key. */
final class QuizzesTest {
  static final String QUIZ = "volumes/vol-fixture/quizzes/ch01-quiz.txt";
  static final String KEY = "volumes/vol-fixture/answer-keys/ch01-answers.txt";

  /** The quiz gate alone on a fixture project's volume: no site is built and no other gate runs. */
  static Quizzes.Checked check(Path p) throws Exception {
    return Quizzes.check(Volume.loadAll(p.resolve("volumes")).get(0));
  }

  // A key carries a program's output, so it must give back exactly what was printed: the rest of the "ANSWER: " line with
  // its own leading spaces (a right-aligned number starts with spaces), each further line with exactly its 10-space indent
  // removed, and a blank output line whether or not an editor kept its 10 spaces. The code above it loses exactly its
  // 8-space indent and keeps the blank line inside it. In a quiz the place to write ends the code, and there is no answer.
  static void testAKeyGivesBackTheCodeAndTheAnswerExactlyAsWritten() {
    String key = String.join("\n",
        " 4. What does this print?",
        "",
        "        int n = 7;",
        "",
        "        IO.println(\"  \" + n);",
        "        IO.println(\"    \" + (n + 1));",
        "        IO.println();",
        "        IO.println();",
        "        IO.println(\"end\");",
        "",
        "      ANSWER:   7",
        "              8",
        "          ",
        "",
        "          end",
        "",
        " 5. Why does a program need main?",
        "");
    List<String> problems = new ArrayList<>();
    var answered = Quizzes.printQuestions("key", key, problems);
    T.eq(List.of(), problems, "a well-formed key raises nothing");
    T.eq(Set.of(4), answered.keySet(), "question 4 is the one print question; question 5 is not one");
    T.eq("int n = 7;\n\nIO.println(\"  \" + n);\nIO.println(\"    \" + (n + 1));\nIO.println();\nIO.println();\nIO.println(\"end\");",
        answered.get(4).code(), "8 spaces off each code line, the blank line inside kept");
    T.eq(1, answered.get(4).answers().size(), "one ANSWER: line");
    T.eq("  7\n    8\n\n\nend", Audit.tidy(answered.get(4).answers().get(0)),
        "the first line's own spaces kept, 10 off each further line, both blank lines kept");
    String quiz = String.join("\n", " 4. What does this print?", "", "        IO.println(1);", "", "    " + "_".repeat(36), "");
    var asked = Quizzes.printQuestions("quiz", quiz, problems);
    T.eq("IO.println(1);", asked.get(4).code(), "the place to write ends the code");
    T.eq(List.of(), asked.get(4).answers(), "and a quiz holds no answer");
  }

  // A question's code is statements, run as main's body, or a whole compact source file with its own void main, run as
  // written: the two forms a quiz writes, and the only two the build runs.
  static void testAStatementSnippetRunsAsMainsBodyAndAWholeProgramAsWritten() {
    T.eq("void main() {\n    int n = 7;\n\n    IO.println(n);\n}\n", Quizzes.program("int n = 7;\n\nIO.println(n);"),
        "statements become main's body, indented 4, the blank line left blank");
    String whole = "int twice(int n) {\n    return n * 2;\n}\n\nvoid main() {\n    IO.println(twice(4));\n}";
    T.eq(whole + "\n", Quizzes.program(whole), "code with its own void main runs as written");
  }

  // Both of the fixture's print questions must run and agree with the key. The count matters as much as the silence: a
  // gate that found no question would report no problem either.
  static void testTheFixturesQuizRunsBothQuestionsAndAgreesWithItsKey() throws Exception {
    var r = check(Fixtures.project("quiz"));
    T.eq(List.of(), r.problems(), "the fixture's quiz agrees with its key");
    T.eq(2, r.questions(), "both print questions ran");
  }

  // An answer is compared as a stated output is (Audit.tidy): CRLF line ends and trailing spaces an editor leaves in the
  // key do not fail a right answer, and a change inside a line does.
  static void testAnAnswerIsComparedAfterTheStatedOutputTidying() throws Exception {
    Path p = Fixtures.project("quiz-tidy");
    Path key = p.resolve(KEY);
    Files.writeString(key, Files.readString(key).replace("          there\n", "          there   \n").replace("\n", "\r\n"));
    T.eq(List.of(), check(p).problems(), "CRLF and trailing spaces in the key are tidied away");
    Files.writeString(key, Files.readString(key).replace("there   ", "the re"));
    T.check(check(p).problems().stream().anyMatch(s -> s.contains("question 2: its key's answer is not what the code prints")),
        "a space inside a line is a difference");
  }

  // Each rule, broken once on the fixture. "where" says which file the edit goes to: the quiz, the key, or both (a change
  // to the code itself, which the key repeats).
  static void testEachQuizRuleFailsWhenBroken() throws Exception {
    record Break(String where, String from, String to, String expect) {}
    var breaks = List.of(
      new Break("key", "ANSWER: 0", "ANSWER: 1", "quizzes/ch01-quiz.txt question 3: its key's answer is not what the code prints"),
      new Break("key", "ANSWER: Hi\n\n          there", "ANSWER: Hi\n          there", "question 2: its key's answer is not what the code prints"),
      new Break("both", "IO.println(a[0]);", "IO.println(a[0])", "question 3: fails to compile"),
      new Break("both", "IO.println(a[0]);", "IO.println(a[3]);", "question 3: uncaught"),
      new Break("both", "IO.println(\"there\");", "IO.println(System.nanoTime());", "question 2: prints something else each run"),
      new Break("both", "IO.println(\"there\");", "IO.println(new int[] {1});", "question 2: prints an identity hash"),
      new Break("key", "        IO.println(\"Hi\");", "        IO.println(\"Ho\");", "question 2: its key shows other code than the quiz"),
      new Break("key", "ANSWER: 0", "ANSWER: 0\n      ANSWER: 0", "question 3: its key has 2 ANSWER: lines, not one"),
      new Break("quiz", " 2. What does this print?", " 2. What does this print, line by line?", "question 2: write it as \"What does this print?\""),
      new Break("quiz", "        IO.println(\"Hi\");\n        IO.println();\n        IO.println(\"there\");",
          "    IO.println(\"Hi\");\n    IO.println();\n    IO.println(\"there\");", "question 2: no code under it"),
      new Break("key", " 3. What does this print?", " 3. What is printed?",
          "quizzes/ch01-quiz.txt question 3: its key has no \"What does this print?\" question 3"),
      new Break("quiz", " 3. What does this print?", " 3. What is printed?",
          "answer-keys/ch01-answers.txt question 3: the quiz has no \"What does this print?\" question 3"),
      new Break("quiz", " 3. What does this print?", " 2. What does this print?", "quizzes/ch01-quiz.txt question 2: the number is used twice"),
      new Break("quiz", "Date: ______________", "Date: ______________\n      ANSWER: Hi", "quizzes/ch01-quiz.txt: line 5 is an ANSWER: line"),
      new Break("quiz", "Chapter 1 - First programs", "Chapter 1 \u2014 First programs", "quizzes/ch01-quiz.txt: line 2 is not plain ASCII"),
      new Break("key", "\nANSWER KEY\n", "\nANSWERS\n", "answer-keys/ch01-answers.txt: does not say ANSWER KEY"));
    for (var b : breaks) {
      Path p = Fixtures.project("quiz-break");
      List<String> files = switch (b.where()) { case "quiz" -> List.of(QUIZ); case "key" -> List.of(KEY); default -> List.of(QUIZ, KEY); };
      for (String f : files) {
        Path file = p.resolve(f);
        String s = Files.readString(file);
        T.check(s.contains(b.from()), "the break applies to " + f + ": " + b.from());
        Files.writeString(file, s.replace(b.from(), b.to()));
      }
      var problems = check(p).problems();
      T.check(problems.stream().anyMatch(x -> x.contains(b.expect())), "\"" + b.expect() + "\" in: " + problems);
    }
  }

  // Every quiz needs its key, every key its quiz, and a quiz needs its chapter: a quiz without its key would publish
  // answers nobody checked, and a key without its quiz checks nothing a reader sees.
  static void testEveryQuizHasItsKeyAndEveryKeyItsQuiz() throws Exception {
    Path p = Fixtures.project("quiz-pairs");
    Path vol = p.resolve("volumes/vol-fixture");
    Files.delete(vol.resolve("answer-keys/ch01-answers.txt"));
    Files.writeString(vol.resolve("answer-keys/ch02-answers.txt"), "ANSWER KEY\n");
    Files.writeString(vol.resolve("quizzes/ch05-quiz.txt"), "GROUNDWORK\n");
    Files.writeString(vol.resolve("quizzes/ch02-types-and-input-quiz.txt"), "GROUNDWORK\n");
    var problems = check(p).problems();
    for (String want : List.of("quizzes/ch01-quiz.txt: has no key answer-keys/ch01-answers.txt",
        "answer-keys/ch02-answers.txt: has no quiz quizzes/ch02-quiz.txt",
        "quizzes/ch05-quiz.txt: there is no chapter 5 for it to belong to",
        "quizzes/ch02-types-and-input-quiz.txt: a quiz is named chNN-quiz.txt"))
      T.check(problems.stream().anyMatch(x -> x.startsWith(want)), want + " in: " + problems);
  }

  // The gate is wired into --check: the fixture passes and says how many questions ran, and a wrong answer fails the run
  // with the question named.
  static void testCheckRunsTheQuizGateAndAWrongAnswerFailsIt() throws Exception {
    Path p = Fixtures.project("quiz-check");
    var r = Fixtures.build(p, "--check", "--no-fork-gate-for-tests");
    T.eq(0, r.exit(), "the fixture passes: " + r.out());
    T.check(r.out().contains("  quizzes: 2 questions run"), "and says how many quiz questions ran: " + r.out());
    Path key = p.resolve(KEY);
    Files.writeString(key, Files.readString(key).replace("ANSWER: 0", "ANSWER: 1"));
    r = Fixtures.build(p, "--check", "--no-fork-gate-for-tests");
    T.eq(1, r.exit(), "a wrong answer fails --check: " + r.out());
    T.check(r.out().contains("  !! quizzes/ch01-quiz.txt question 3: its key's answer is not what the code prints"), r.out());
  }
}
```

```java
  /** The vocabulary gate alone on a fixture project's volume. */
  private static List<String> problems(Path p) throws Exception {
    Volume v = Volume.loadAll(p.resolve("volumes")).get(0);
    Map<Page, List<Box>> boxes = new LinkedHashMap<>();
    for (Page page : v.pages()) boxes.put(page, Boxes.of(v, page));
    return Vocabulary.check(v, boxes);
  }
  static void testAQuizQuestionMayNotUseWhatOnlyALaterChapterTeaches() throws Exception {
    // DESIGN section 4 holds a quiz to its chapter as it holds a solution, forward: chapter 1's quiz may not use
    // Integer.parseInt, which the fixture first teaches in chapter 2 (ch02-types-and-input#1). The question's code is
    // a statement snippet, so this also proves the gate reads the code as it runs, wrapped in main.
    Path p = Fixtures.project("vocab-quiz-forward");
    Path quiz = p.resolve("volumes/vol-fixture/quizzes/ch01-quiz.txt");
    Files.writeString(quiz, Files.readString(quiz).replace("IO.println(\"there\");", "IO.println(Integer.parseInt(\"1\"));"));
    List<String> problems = problems(p);
    T.check(problems.stream().anyMatch(s -> s.startsWith("quizzes/ch01-quiz.txt question 2 (quiz): uses what the course has not shown by chapter 1")
        && s.contains("member java.lang.Integer#parseInt")), "names the quiz, the question and the item: " + problems);
  }
  static void testAChapterTwoQuizMayUseWhatChapterOneTaught() throws Exception {
    // Backward: chapter 2's quiz is held to chapters 1 and 2 together. int[] is taught in chapter 1 alone
    // (ch01-first-programs#2). The same quiz first uses Math.max, which no chapter teaches, and must fail, so a quiz the
    // gate never read cannot pass this test. Vocabulary reads only the quiz, so this chapter 2 quiz needs no key here.
    Path p = Fixtures.project("vocab-quiz-backward");
    Path quiz = p.resolve("volumes/vol-fixture/quizzes/ch02-quiz.txt");
    String body = " 1. What does this print?\n\n        int[] a = new int[] {Integer.parseInt(\"4\")};\n        IO.println(%s);\n";
    Files.writeString(quiz, body.formatted("Math.max(a[0], 2)"));
    List<String> problems = problems(p);
    T.check(problems.stream().anyMatch(s -> s.startsWith("quizzes/ch02-quiz.txt question 1 (quiz)") && s.contains("member java.lang.Math#max")),
        "the gate read chapter 2's quiz: " + problems);
    Files.writeString(quiz, body.formatted("a[0] * 2"));
    problems = problems(p);
    T.check(problems.stream().noneMatch(s -> s.contains("ch02-quiz")), "int[] from chapter 1 is known to chapter 2's quiz: " + problems);
  }
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement.**

  (a) Create build/Quizzes.java:

```java
/*
 *  Copyright 2026 Groundwork contributors.
 *
 *  Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
 *  you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
 *  You may obtain a copy of the License at
 *
 *       http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
 *
 *  Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
 *  distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
 *  WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
 *  See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
 *  limitations under the License.
 */
import java.io.IOException;
import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;
import java.nio.file.Files;
import java.nio.file.Path;
import java.time.Duration;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.HashSet;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Locale;
import java.util.Map;
import java.util.Set;
import java.util.TreeMap;
import java.util.regex.Matcher;
import java.util.regex.Pattern;

/** DESIGN section 6: a quiz per chapter, plain text, every "What does this print?" answer verified on the real JDK, answer
 *  keys never published. quizzes/chNN-quiz.txt is chapter NN's quiz and answer-keys/chNN-answers.txt its key, both in the
 *  Python course's layout (volumes/README.md, "Quizzes"). check pairs every quiz with its key and every key with its quiz,
 *  and runs the code of every "What does this print?" question on the pinned JDK, which must print exactly the key's
 *  answer once both are tidied as a stated output is (Audit.tidy). Vocabulary.check holds the same code to its chapter.
 *  Every other question (a term, code with a blank in it, a reason) is read by the review, not run. */
final class Quizzes {
  private Quizzes() {}

  static final Pattern QUIZ_NAME = Pattern.compile("ch(\\d+)-quiz\\.txt");
  private static final Pattern KEY_NAME = Pattern.compile("ch(\\d+)-answers\\.txt");
  /** A question's first line: its number right-aligned in two columns (" 5. ", "12. "), then its text. */
  private static final Pattern QUESTION = Pattern.compile(" ?(\\d+)\\. (.*)");
  /** An answer in a key: "ANSWER:", one space, then the answer's first line exactly as printed (any more spaces are its own). */
  private static final Pattern ANSWER = Pattern.compile(" *ANSWER:(?: (.*))?");
  static final String PRINT = "What does this print?";
  private static final String CODE_INDENT = " ".repeat(8);
  private static final String ANSWER_INDENT = " ".repeat(10);
  private static final Duration DEADLINE = Duration.ofSeconds(10);
  /** Five runs, not two: a small Set.of or Map.of agrees with itself across two JVM starts about a quarter of the time. */
  private static final int RUNS = 5;
  /** An identity hash (an array's or a plain object's toString) repeats across starts of one JDK but is promised nowhere. */
  private static final Pattern IDENTITY_HASH = Pattern.compile("@[0-9a-f]{5,8}\\b");

  /** One "What does this print?" question: its number, its code with the 8-space indent removed (no trailing newline),
   *  and each answer under it, its lines joined with "\n" (none in a quiz). */
  record Question(int number, String code, List<String> answers) {}

  /** What check found: how many questions it ran, and every problem. */
  record Checked(int questions, List<String> problems) {}

  /** Every quiz of v paired with its key, every key with its quiz, and every "What does this print?" question run; how
   *  many questions ran, and each problem as "<file> question N: <problem>" or "<file>: <problem>", in file order. */
  static Checked check(Volume v) throws BuildError {
    List<String> problems = new ArrayList<>();
    int ran = 0;
    for (Path quiz : v.quizzes()) {
      String name = quiz.getFileName().toString(), where = "quizzes/" + name;
      Matcher m = QUIZ_NAME.matcher(name);
      if (!m.matches()) {
        problems.add(where + ": a quiz is named chNN-quiz.txt, NN being its chapter's number as the chapter's file writes it");
        continue;
      }
      int chapter = Integer.parseInt(m.group(1));
      if (chapterPage(v, chapter) == null) {
        problems.add(where + ": there is no chapter " + chapter + " for it to belong to");
        continue;
      }
      String keyWhere = "answer-keys/ch" + m.group(1) + "-answers.txt";
      Path key = v.dir().resolve(keyWhere);
      if (!Files.isRegularFile(key)) {
        problems.add(where + ": has no key " + keyWhere);
        continue;
      }
      String quizText = text(quiz), keyText = text(key);
      String[] lines = quizText.split("\n", -1);
      for (int i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
        if (lines[i].chars().anyMatch(c -> c > 127)) {
          problems.add(where + ": line " + (i + 1) + " is not plain ASCII; a .txt cannot say its encoding, so a reader's phone may garble it");
          break;
        }
      }
      for (int i = 0; i < lines.length; i++)
        if (ANSWER.matcher(lines[i]).matches()) problems.add(where + ": line " + (i + 1) + " is an ANSWER: line; answers live only in " + keyWhere);
      if (!keyText.contains("ANSWER KEY"))
        problems.add(keyWhere + ": does not say ANSWER KEY, the words the publishing guard refuses to publish");
      Map<Integer, Question> asked = printQuestions(where, quizText, problems);
      Map<Integer, Question> answered = printQuestions(keyWhere, keyText, problems);
      for (int n : answered.keySet())
        if (!asked.containsKey(n)) problems.add(keyWhere + " question " + n + ": the quiz has no \"" + PRINT + "\" question " + n);
      for (Question q : asked.values()) {
        String id = where + " question " + q.number();
        Question k = answered.get(q.number());
        if (k == null) {
          problems.add(id + ": its key has no \"" + PRINT + "\" question " + q.number());
          continue;
        }
        if (!k.code().equals(q.code())) {
          problems.add(id + ": its key shows other code than the quiz");
          continue;
        }
        if (k.answers().size() != 1) {
          problems.add(id + ": its key has " + k.answers().size() + " ANSWER: lines, not one (the output's further lines go under it, indented 10 spaces)");
          continue;
        }
        ran++;
        problems.addAll(run(id, q.code(), k.answers().get(0), Path.of("build/.work/quizzes/" + name.replace(".txt", "") + "-" + q.number())));
      }
    }
    Path keys = v.dir().resolve("answer-keys");
    if (Files.isDirectory(keys)) {
      List<Path> files;
      try (var s = Files.list(keys)) {
        files = s.sorted().toList();
      } catch (IOException e) {
        throw new BuildError(keys + ": " + e.getMessage());
      }
      for (Path k : files) {
        Matcher m = KEY_NAME.matcher(k.getFileName().toString());
        if (m.matches() && !Files.isRegularFile(v.dir().resolve("quizzes/ch" + m.group(1) + "-quiz.txt")))
          problems.add("answer-keys/" + k.getFileName() + ": has no quiz quizzes/ch" + m.group(1) + "-quiz.txt");
      }
    }
    return new Checked(ran, problems);
  }

  /** Every "What does this print?" question in text, a quiz's or a key's, by number. A question starts at a line " N. "
   *  (N right-aligned in two columns). A print question's line is exactly " N. What does this print?"; its code is the
   *  lines under it that are blank or indented 8 spaces, from the first indented one, the 8 spaces removed and blank lines
   *  at its end dropped (a blank line inside it stays). In a key, each "ANSWER: " line before the next question is one
   *  answer: the rest of that line, then each line under it that is blank or indented 10 spaces, the 10 removed, up to the
   *  first line that is neither. Adds to problems a number used twice, a print question with no code, and a question that
   *  says "does this print" in other words, which would otherwise be skipped without a word. */
  static Map<Integer, Question> printQuestions(String where, String text, List<String> problems) {
    String[] lines = text.replace("\r\n", "\n").split("\n", -1);
    Map<Integer, Question> found = new TreeMap<>();
    Set<Integer> numbers = new HashSet<>();
    for (int i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
      Matcher q = QUESTION.matcher(lines[i]);
      if (!q.matches()) continue;
      int n = Integer.parseInt(q.group(1));
      if (!numbers.add(n)) {
        problems.add(where + " question " + n + ": the number is used twice");
        continue;
      }
      if (!q.group(2).equals(PRINT)) {
        if (q.group(2).toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT).contains("does this print"))
          problems.add(where + " question " + n + ": write it as \"" + PRINT + "\" alone on its line, or the build cannot find its code");
        continue;
      }
      int j = i + 1;
      while (j < lines.length && lines[j].isBlank()) j++;
      List<String> code = new ArrayList<>();
      for (; j < lines.length && (lines[j].isBlank() || lines[j].startsWith(CODE_INDENT)); j++)
        code.add(lines[j].isBlank() ? "" : lines[j].substring(CODE_INDENT.length()));
      while (!code.isEmpty() && code.get(code.size() - 1).isEmpty()) code.remove(code.size() - 1);
      if (code.isEmpty()) {
        problems.add(where + " question " + n + ": no code under it, indented 8 spaces");
        continue;
      }
      List<String> answers = new ArrayList<>();
      while (j < lines.length && !QUESTION.matcher(lines[j]).matches()) {
        Matcher a = ANSWER.matcher(lines[j++]);
        if (!a.matches()) continue;
        List<String> answer = new ArrayList<>(List.of(a.group(1) == null ? "" : a.group(1)));
        for (; j < lines.length && (lines[j].isBlank() || lines[j].startsWith(ANSWER_INDENT)); j++)
          answer.add(lines[j].isBlank() ? "" : lines[j].substring(ANSWER_INDENT.length()));
        answers.add(String.join("\n", answer));
      }
      found.put(n, new Question(n, String.join("\n", code), answers));
      i = j - 1;
    }
    return found;
  }

  /** The compact source file a question's code runs as. Code with a line that starts "void main(" at its own margin is a
   *  whole program and runs as written; any other code is statements, run as main's body, each line indented 4 spaces (a
   *  blank line stays blank). A question that needs a method or an import beside main is written whole. */
  static String program(String code) {
    String[] lines = code.split("\n", -1);
    for (String l : lines) if (l.startsWith("void main(")) return code + "\n";
    StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder("void main() {\n");
    for (String l : lines) sb.append(l.isEmpty() ? "" : "    " + l).append('\n');
    return sb.append("}\n").toString();
  }

  /** A quiz's or key's text, CRLF read as LF. Bytes that are not UTF-8 decode as U+FFFD rather than throwing, so a quiz
   *  saved in another encoding is reported as not ASCII instead of stopping --check. */
  static String text(Path file) throws BuildError {
    try {
      return new String(Files.readAllBytes(file), StandardCharsets.UTF_8).replace("\r\n", "\n");
    } catch (IOException e) {
      throw new BuildError(file + ": " + e.getMessage());
    }
  }

  /** Chapter n's own content page in v (not its practice page), or null when v has no chapter n. */
  static Page chapterPage(Volume v, int n) {
    for (Page p : v.pages()) if (p.chapter() == n && !p.practice()) return p;
    return null;
  }

  /** Compiles a question's program on the pinned JDK and runs it five times with no input. It must compile, run to its
   *  end, print the same every time (an answer that changes from run to run cannot be verified), print no identity hash,
   *  and print exactly answer, both tidied as a stated output is. */
  private static List<String> run(String id, String code, String answer, Path outDir) throws BuildError {
    Jdk.Compiled compiled = Jdk.compile(program(code), outDir);
    if (!compiled.ok())
      return List.of(id + ": fails to compile: " + (compiled.errors().isEmpty() ? "javac reported no error" : compiled.errors().get(0).message()));
    Jdk.Run first = Jdk.run(compiled.classes(), "", DEADLINE);
    if (!first.status().equals("ok")) return List.of(id + ": " + first.status() + " (a quiz question's code must run to its end)");
    for (int i = 1; i < RUNS; i++)
      if (!first.equals(Jdk.run(compiled.classes(), "", DEADLINE)))
        return List.of(id + ": prints something else each run, so no answer to it can be verified");
    if (IDENTITY_HASH.matcher(first.stdout()).find())
      return List.of(id + ": prints an identity hash (such as [I@1b6d3586), which no JDK promises to repeat");
    String want = Audit.tidy(answer), got = Audit.tidy(first.stdout());
    if (!want.equals(got))
      return List.of(id + ": its key's answer is not what the code prints\n      expected: " + show(want) + "\n      actual:   " + show(got));
    return List.of();
  }

  private static String show(String s) { return s.replace("\n", "\\n"); }
}
```

  "`practice/`, `quizzes/`, `answer-keys/` and `content/_solutions.md` are all optional (a quiz and its key come
  together):". Insert before `## The vocabulary gate` (:139):

````markdown
## Quizzes

`quizzes/chNN-quiz.txt` is chapter NN's quiz, published as it is, and `answer-keys/chNN-answers.txt` is its key, never
published; NN is written as the chapter's own file writes it (`ch01`). Every quiz needs its key, every key its quiz, and a
quiz needs its chapter. Both are plain text in the Python course's layout: `GROUNDWORK`, `Chapter N - <title>`, then the
quiz's `Name:` and `Date:` blanks or the key's line `ANSWER KEY` (the words the publishing guard refuses to publish, so a
key that reached `site/` by mistake would stop the build); then the sections `VOCABULARY`, `READING CODE`, `WRITING CODE`
and `WHY IT WORKS`, each underlined with hyphens. A question starts at the margin with its number right-aligned in two
columns (` 5. `, `12. `), its further lines are indented four spaces, and lines wrap at 74 columns. In the key, each blank's
answer follows its question on its own line, `      ANSWER: <answer>` (six spaces). A quiz is plain ASCII, because a
`.txt` cannot say its encoding and a phone may garble anything else: `-` for a dash, `'` and `"` for curly quotes, `...`
for an ellipsis. A quiz never holds an `ANSWER:` line.

**"What does this print?"** A question whose line is exactly ` N. What does this print?` is run. Its code is the lines
under it indented eight spaces (a blank line inside the code is kept), and in the quiz the place to write follows, four
spaces and 36 underscores. In the key the same question and the same code are followed by one `ANSWER:` line holding what
the program prints: the output's first line after `ANSWER: ` (one space; any further leading spaces are the program's
own), each further line under it indented ten spaces, and a blank line of output as a blank line:

```text
 6. What does this print?

        IO.println("A");
        IO.println();
        IO.println("B");

    ____________________________________
```

```text
 6. What does this print?

        IO.println("A");
        IO.println();
        IO.println("B");

      ANSWER: A

          B
```

Code like this, statements, runs as `main`'s body (`void main() {`, the code indented four spaces, `}`). When a line of the
code starts `void main(` at the code's own margin, the code is a whole compact source file and runs as written; code that
needs a method or an import beside `main` is written that way. `--check` compiles each such question on the pinned JDK,
runs it five times with no input, and requires it to run to its end, print the same every time, print no identity hash,
and print exactly the key's
answer, both tidied as a stated output is (above). The key must show the quiz's code, so the two cannot drift apart
unnoticed, and a question that asks "does this print" in any other words is reported rather than skipped. The code is
also held to the vocabulary gate (below). Every other question (a term, code with a blank in it, a reason) is neither run
nor held to the vocabulary gate: the review reads it.

````

```text
feat(build): every quiz's "What does this print?" runs on the pinned JDK against its key (Plan 5 Task 7)

DESIGN section 6 promises a quiz per chapter with every "what does this print" answer verified on the real JDK and keys
never published, and section 4 holds quizzes to the chapters' vocabulary. Until now a quiz was only copied and a key only
guarded; nothing read either. The format is the Python course's: plain ASCII, numbered questions, a key with ANSWER KEY
and ANSWER: lines. A question whose line is exactly "What does this print?" has its code indented 8 spaces; statements
run as main's body, code with its own void main runs whole. --check compiles it, runs it five times with no input, and
requires the key's single ANSWER: (further lines indented 10) to equal the output after the stated-output tidying. The
key must repeat the quiz's code, every quiz needs its key and every key its quiz, a quiz must be ASCII and hold no
ANSWER: line, and a key must say ANSWER KEY so the publishing guard catches it anywhere under site/. The same code is
held to its chapter's vocabulary (chapter N's quiz: chapters 1 to N). The fixture's chapter 1 quiz now has two such
questions (a snippet with a blank output line, a whole program with a blank code line) and a key in the format.
--check prints "quizzes: N questions run".

Breaks, each restored and compared with cmp:
- the demo project's key answer 0 -> 1: --check exit 1, naming question 3 (exit <n>)
- ANSWER_INDENT 10 -> 6 spaces: QuizzesTest exit <n>
- answer and output compared untidied: QuizzesTest exit <n>
- snippets not wrapped in main: QuizzesTest exit <n>
- the code stops at its first blank line: QuizzesTest exit <n>
- the quiz loop removed from Vocabulary: VocabularyTest exit <n>
- the quiz gate's problems dropped in Build.run: QuizzesTest.testCheckRuns... exit <n>
- the fixture key's answer 0 -> 1: AuditTest.testTheFixturePassesCheck exit <n>
BuildTest <n> tests, 0 failed.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
```

  `git log -1 --format=%B | tail -1` prints the Co-Authored-By line and nothing follows it. Delete
  build/.work/commit-msg.txt afterwards.
