### Task 8: Authoring speed: --check on one chapter, and its time

`--check` is serial and re-audits every box of every volume on each run (Build.java:93-158; C-machinery section 4, risks
5 and 13): per box an in-process javac compile, a second javac pass for input use, up to two fresh JVMs, `Vocab.of`, and
in the fork gate a TeaVM compile and a JDK run under Node. The course will hold about 170 chapter boxes plus practice boxes,
quiz questions and the checks' three-way proofs, and nothing has been measured. This task adds the authoring loop's
command, `$J build/Build.java --check --only ch03`, which runs every gate on chapter 3's pages and its quiz alone, and
makes every `--check` end with each gate's time. A plain `--check` still runs everything, and is all that CI and Pages
run.

**What `--only <name>` selects.** Each page whose slug is `<name>` or starts `<name>-`: `ch03` is chapter 3's content page
and its practice page (`ch03-<slug>`, `ch03-<slug>-practice`), and so is `ch03-<slug>`, since the practice page's slug
starts with it: the unit is the chapter, whose quiz belongs to it. `ch03-<slug>-practice` is the practice page alone (no
quiz); `99-appendices` an appendix. On those pages: the audit of their boxes; the vocabulary gate on their practice boxes
and on the solutions and starters keyed to their boxes; the quiz of a chapter whose content page is selected; the fork gate on
their examples; and the checks' three-way proofs of their boxes. The vocabulary of every chapter is still derived from
all its boxes (Vocabulary's `knownByChapter`, unchanged), so a page checked alone is held to what the chapters before it
taught. `--only` needs `--check` (exit 2 otherwise); a name no page has is a build error (exit 1), never a vacuous pass. A
quiz or key that belongs to no page (misnamed, or for a chapter the volume lacks) is reported only by a run that selects
every page. A selection with no example skips the fork gate and says so; a plain `--check` with none still fails, as
today.

**The budget, today** (nothing in this task changes it). `.github/workflows/pages.yml`'s build job has
`timeout-minutes: 30` (:31) for the checkout, `bash ci/setup.sh` (the pinned JDK and both runtime releases, downloaded) and
the full `"$J" build/Build.java --check` (:37-38); its deploy job has 15 (:45). `.github/workflows/quick.yml`'s node job
has `timeout-minutes: 60` (:30) for `npm ci`, `ci/setup.sh` and `ci/quick.sh node`, which runs BuildTest (itself many
fixture `--check`s), every runtime, web and jshell test under Node, and last `build-check`, the full `--check`
(ci/lib.sh:78-90); its chromium job has 60 (:41) and leaks 15 (:52). weekly.yml's jobs have 120. The fork gate stops itself
at 30 minutes (build/ForkGate.java:36), the whole of the Pages job, so on Pages the job would be killed before that
deadline could speak. **If a full `--check`'s `all gates` passes 20 minutes** (on the Mac, or in a quick node or Pages
run's log; the runner is the slower of the two), stop after that chapter's task, before the next chapter, and bring the
author the `gate times:` lines from both. Do not raise a timeout or a deadline, parallelize a gate or split the check on
your own: it is the author's decision, later. The choices to put: (a) raise pages.yml's build timeout, and ForkGate's
30-minute deadline with it; (b) run the audit's boxes on a few threads (each box is its own JVM and its own
`build/.work/classes/<id>`; problems must still print in page order); (c) the same inside `runtime/test/examples.mjs`
for the fork gate; (d) give `build-check` a CI job of its own. Twenty minutes leaves a third of the Pages job for
checkout, setup's downloads and a slow runner.

  verdict(s)` line); Task 6's `examplesOf` with `raises`; the fixture as Tasks 5-7 leave it (Step 1's comments count it).
- Produces:
  - `java build/Build.java --check --only <name>`; `usage()` reads

  - `Predicate<Page> only` as the last parameter of `Audit.check(List<Volume>, Map<Page, List<Box>>, Predicate<Page>)`,
    `Vocabulary.check(Volume, Map<Page, List<Box>>, Predicate<Page>)`, `Quizzes.check(Volume, Predicate<Page>)`,
    `Proofs.of(Volume, Map<Page, List<Box>>, List<String>, Predicate<Page>)` and Build's private
    `examplesOf(Map<Page, List<Box>>, Predicate<Page>)`; a gate reports on a box only when `only.test(<its page>)`, and
    on a quiz only when `only.test(Quizzes.chapterPage(v, <its chapter>))`.
  - The three-way proof gate (Task 5), narrowed like the others: `Proofs.of` applies its count rule to, and puts in
    proofs.json, only the checked boxes on the pages `only` selects; `ForkGate.runProofs` is unchanged, since it
    proves whatever proofs.json holds; the proof still runs where Task 5 put it, after the fork gate and only without
    `--no-fork-gate-for-tests`, and is timed there as `proofs`. Narrowing it is sound: a check belongs to one box on one
    page, exactly as an example does, and a plain `--check` (CI, Pages) still proves every check.
  - Output lines: `gate times: audit <s> s, vocabulary <s> s, quizzes <s> s[, fork gate <s> s][, proofs <s> s]; all gates <s> s`
    (`<s>` one decimal, `Locale.ROOT`; the proof gate's count rule, which runs nothing, is timed with the vocabulary
    gate; `fork gate` and `proofs` appear when they ran), always the last line of a `--check` that reached the gates,
    passing or failing; `<c> checks, <a> answers judged, <w> wrong verdict(s)` counts the selected pages' checks alone;
    `  fork gate: no example on the page(s) --only <name> selects`; and on misuse `--only works only with --check: it
    names the pages every gate checks` (exit 2) or `build: --only <name>: no page is named <name> or starts <name>-`
    (exit 1).

    r = Fixtures.build(p, "--check", "--no-fork-gate-for-tests");

    T.check(times.matcher(r.out()).find(), "printed when a gate fails too: " + r.out());
  }

```java
        Jdk.checkPinned();
        List<String> problems = Audit.check(volumes, allBoxes);
        for (Volume v : volumes) problems.addAll(Vocabulary.check(v, boxesByVolume.get(v)));
        // DESIGN section 3: what each check's three-way proof needs, and whether _solutions.md gives it, needs no
        // runtime; the proof itself runs below, after the fork gate.
        List<Object> proofs = new ArrayList<>();
        for (Volume v : volumes) proofs.addAll(Proofs.of(v, boxesByVolume.get(v), problems));
        int quizQuestions = 0;
        for (Volume v : volumes) {
          Quizzes.Checked quizzes = Quizzes.check(v);
          quizQuestions += quizzes.questions();
          problems.addAll(quizzes.problems());
        }
        long checked = 0, stated = 0;
        for (Volume v : volumes) {
          for (Page p : v.pages()) {
            long n = allBoxes.get(p).stream().filter(b -> !b.decl().reference()).count();
            out.println("  " + p.slug() + ": " + n + " boxes checked");
            checked += n;
            stated += allBoxes.get(p).stream().filter(Audit::statesComparedOutput).count();
          }
        }
        out.println("  quizzes: " + quizQuestions + " questions run");
        int exampleCount = 0, differing = 0, wrongVerdicts = 0;
        if (!noForkGate) {
          List<Object> examples = examplesOf(allBoxes);
          exampleCount = examples.size();
          Path examplesJson = project.resolve("build/.work/examples.json");
          writeJson(examplesJson, examples);
          List<String> forkProblems = ForkGate.run(tools, examplesJson, site.resolve("runtime"));
          differing = forkProblems.size();
          problems.addAll(forkProblems);
          // Every answer judged by the page's own check.js on both runtimes. A volume with no check has nothing to
          // prove, and runtime/test/proofs.mjs refuses an empty list.
          if (!proofs.isEmpty()) {
            Path proofsJson = project.resolve("build/.work/proofs.json");
            writeJson(proofsJson, proofs);
            List<String> proofProblems = ForkGate.runProofs(tools, proofsJson, site.resolve("runtime"));
            wrongVerdicts = proofProblems.size();
            problems.addAll(proofProblems);
          }
        }
        for (String problem : problems) out.println("  !! " + problem);
        out.println(checked + " boxes checked, " + stated + " stated outputs compared, " + problems.size() + " problem(s)");
        if (!noForkGate) {
          out.println(exampleCount + " examples, " + differing + " differing");
          out.println(proofs.size() + " checks, " + Proofs.answers(proofs) + " answers judged, " + wrongVerdicts + " wrong verdict(s)");
        }

        for (Volume v : volumes) problems.addAll(Vocabulary.check(v, boxesByVolume.get(v), only));

            long n = allBoxes.get(p).stream().filter(b -> !b.decl().reference()).count();

            stated += allBoxes.get(p).stream().filter(Audit::statesComparedOutput).count();

          exampleCount = examples.size();

  After `usage()`, add:

```java
  /** --only's choice of pages: with no name, every page; with a name, each page whose slug is that name or starts with it
   *  and a hyphen, so "ch03" is chapter 3's content page and its practice page (ch03-<slug>, ch03-<slug>-practice). */
  private static Predicate<Page> selection(String name) {
    if (name == null) return p -> true;
    return p -> p.slug().equals(name) || p.slug().startsWith(name + "-");
  }

  /** Records under gate the milliseconds since start, and returns now: where the next gate's time starts. */
  private static long lap(Map<String, Long> times, String gate, long start) {
    long now = System.nanoTime();
    times.put(gate, (now - start) / 1_000_000);
    return now;
  }

  /** The line --check ends with: "gate times: audit 1.2 s, vocabulary 0.4 s, quizzes 0.9 s, fork gate 8.0 s, proofs
   *  5.1 s; all gates 15.7 s", each gate's time in the order it ran, then all of them together. */
  private static String gateTimes(Map<String, Long> times, long totalNanos) {
    List<String> parts = new ArrayList<>();
    for (var e : times.entrySet()) parts.add(e.getKey() + " " + seconds(e.getValue()));
    return "gate times: " + String.join(", ", parts) + "; all gates " + seconds(totalNanos / 1_000_000);
  }

  private static String seconds(long millis) { return String.format(Locale.ROOT, "%.1f s", millis / 1000.0); }
```

  Replace `examplesOf` as Task 6 left it (:224-240 at 3190510; the lines Tasks 5 and 7 added above it move it down),

```java
  /** The fork gate's input: every non-reference box (compileError boxes included: both sides must fail to
   *  compile the same way), as {id, src, stdin, varies, raises}, in page order (raises lets
   *  runtime/test/examples.mjs leave a declared runaway recursion's frames out). */
  private static List<Object> examplesOf(Map<Page, List<Box>> allBoxes) {
    List<Object> examples = new ArrayList<>();
    for (var boxes : allBoxes.values()) {
      for (Box b : boxes) {
        if (b.decl().reference()) continue;
        Map<String, Object> entry = new LinkedHashMap<>();
        entry.put("id", b.id());
        entry.put("src", b.source());
        entry.put("stdin", b.decl().stdin().isEmpty() ? "" : String.join("\n", b.decl().stdin()) + "\n");
        entry.put("varies", b.decl().varies());
        entry.put("raises", b.decl().raises());
        examples.add(entry);
      }
    }
    return examples;
  }

  with this, which keeps Task 6's `raises` (runtime/test/examples.mjs reads it, and without it
  ForkGateTest.testTheFixtureAgreesOnBothRuntimes fails on ch02-types-and-input#5):

```java
  /** The fork gate's input: every non-reference box (compileError boxes included: both sides must fail to
   *  compile the same way) on a page only selects, as {id, src, stdin, varies, raises}, in page order (raises lets
   *  runtime/test/examples.mjs leave a declared runaway recursion's frames out). */
  private static List<Object> examplesOf(Map<Page, List<Box>> allBoxes, Predicate<Page> only) {
    List<Object> examples = new ArrayList<>();
    for (var e : allBoxes.entrySet()) {
      if (!only.test(e.getKey())) continue;
      for (Box b : e.getValue()) {
        if (b.decl().reference()) continue;
        Map<String, Object> entry = new LinkedHashMap<>();
        entry.put("id", b.id());
        entry.put("src", b.source());
        entry.put("stdin", b.decl().stdin().isEmpty() ? "" : String.join("\n", b.decl().stdin()) + "\n");
        entry.put("varies", b.decl().varies());
        entry.put("raises", b.decl().raises());
        examples.add(entry);
      }
    }
    return examples;
  }

```java
  /** Compiles and runs every non-reference box of every page only selects (every page, unless --only names some) of
   *  every volume, applying the five rules; returns each problem as "<box id>: <problem>", in page order. A box's own
   *  directory under build/.work/classes/ is named after its id, with '#' turned into '_'. */
  static List<String> check(List<Volume> volumes, Map<Page, List<Box>> allBoxes, Predicate<Page> only) throws BuildError {
    List<String> problems = new ArrayList<>();
    for (Volume v : volumes) {
      for (Page p : v.pages()) {
        if (!only.test(p)) continue;
        for (Box box : allBoxes.get(p)) {
          if (box.decl().reference()) continue;
          problems.addAll(checkBox(box));
        }
      }
    }
    return problems;
  }

  (d) build/Quizzes.java. Add `import java.util.function.Predicate;` before `import java.util.regex.Matcher;`. Replace
  `check` (its comment and body) with:

```java
  /** Every quiz of v paired with its key, every key with its quiz, and every "What does this print?" question run, for the
   *  chapters whose content page only selects (every page, unless --only names some); how many questions ran, and each
   *  problem as "<file> question N: <problem>" or "<file>: <problem>", in file order. A quiz or key that belongs to no
   *  page (misnamed, or for a chapter v lacks) is reported only when only selects every page, a plain --check: --only
   *  cannot name a page it belongs to. */
  static Checked check(Volume v, Predicate<Page> only) throws BuildError {
    boolean everything = v.pages().stream().allMatch(only);
    List<String> problems = new ArrayList<>();
    int ran = 0;
    for (Path quiz : v.quizzes()) {
      String name = quiz.getFileName().toString(), where = "quizzes/" + name;
      Matcher m = QUIZ_NAME.matcher(name);
      if (!m.matches()) {
        if (everything) problems.add(where + ": a quiz is named chNN-quiz.txt, NN being its chapter's number as the chapter's file writes it");
        continue;
      }
      int chapter = Integer.parseInt(m.group(1));
      Page page = chapterPage(v, chapter);
      if (page == null) {
        if (everything) problems.add(where + ": there is no chapter " + chapter + " for it to belong to");
        continue;
      }
      if (!only.test(page)) continue;
      String keyWhere = "answer-keys/ch" + m.group(1) + "-answers.txt";
      Path key = v.dir().resolve(keyWhere);
      if (!Files.isRegularFile(key)) {
        problems.add(where + ": has no key " + keyWhere);
        continue;
      }
      String quizText = text(quiz), keyText = text(key);
      String[] lines = quizText.split("\n", -1);
      for (int i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
        if (lines[i].chars().anyMatch(c -> c > 127)) {
          problems.add(where + ": line " + (i + 1) + " is not plain ASCII; a .txt cannot say its encoding, so a reader's phone may garble it");
          break;
        }
      }
      for (int i = 0; i < lines.length; i++)
        if (ANSWER.matcher(lines[i]).matches()) problems.add(where + ": line " + (i + 1) + " is an ANSWER: line; answers live only in " + keyWhere);
      if (!keyText.contains("ANSWER KEY"))
        problems.add(keyWhere + ": does not say ANSWER KEY, the words the publishing guard refuses to publish");
      Map<Integer, Question> asked = printQuestions(where, quizText, problems);
      Map<Integer, Question> answered = printQuestions(keyWhere, keyText, problems);
      for (int n : answered.keySet())
        if (!asked.containsKey(n)) problems.add(keyWhere + " question " + n + ": the quiz has no \"" + PRINT + "\" question " + n);
      for (Question q : asked.values()) {
        String id = where + " question " + q.number();
        Question k = answered.get(q.number());
        if (k == null) {
          problems.add(id + ": its key has no \"" + PRINT + "\" question " + q.number());
          continue;
        }
        if (!k.code().equals(q.code())) {
          problems.add(id + ": its key shows other code than the quiz");
          continue;
        }
        if (k.answers().size() != 1) {
          problems.add(id + ": its key has " + k.answers().size() + " ANSWER: lines, not one (the output's further lines go under it, indented 10 spaces)");
          continue;
        }
        ran++;
        problems.addAll(run(id, q.code(), k.answers().get(0), Path.of("build/.work/quizzes/" + name.replace(".txt", "") + "-" + q.number())));
      }
    }
    Path keys = v.dir().resolve("answer-keys");
    if (Files.isDirectory(keys)) {
      List<Path> files;
      try (var s = Files.list(keys)) {
        files = s.sorted().toList();
      } catch (IOException e) {
        throw new BuildError(keys + ": " + e.getMessage());
      }
      for (Path k : files) {
        Matcher m = KEY_NAME.matcher(k.getFileName().toString());
        if (!m.matches() || Files.isRegularFile(v.dir().resolve("quizzes/ch" + m.group(1) + "-quiz.txt"))) continue;
        Page page = chapterPage(v, Integer.parseInt(m.group(1)));
        if (everything || page != null && only.test(page))
          problems.add("answer-keys/" + k.getFileName() + ": has no quiz quizzes/ch" + m.group(1) + "-quiz.txt");
      }
    }
    return new Checked(ran, problems);
  }
```

gate <s> s, proofs <s> s; all gates <s> s`: the number to watch against the Pages build job's 30 minutes.

feat(build): --check --only <page> for the authoring loop, and every --check ends with each gate's time (Plan 5 Task 8)

--check re-audits every box of every volume on each run, one box at a time, and the course will hold about 170 chapter
boxes, the practice boxes, the quizzes and the checks' proofs. --only <name> runs every gate on the pages whose slug is
<name> or starts <name>- (ch03: the chapter's page and its practice page) and on that chapter's quiz alone: the audit,
the vocabulary gate, the quiz gate, the fork gate and the checks' three-way proof (Proofs.of: its count rule and the
checks it sends to be proved) each take the same page filter. Every chapter's vocabulary is still
derived from all its boxes, so a page checked alone is held to the chapters before it. --only needs --check, a name no
page has fails, a quiz or key belonging to no page is reported only by a run of every page, and a selection with nothing
to run skips the fork gate and says so (a plain --check with none still fails). A plain --check is unchanged and is what
CI and Pages run.

Every --check now ends with "gate times: audit ..., vocabulary ..., quizzes ..., fork gate ..., proofs ...; all gates
...". The Pages
build job has 30 minutes for checkout, ci/setup.sh and the full --check, quick's node job 60 for everything; past 20
minutes for all gates, the author decides what changes. On the fixture (Mac): <the three gate-times lines>.

Breaks, each restored and compared with cmp:
- --only selecting every page: AuditTest.testTheOnlyFlagChecks... exit <n>
- chapter 1 left out of the vocabulary derivation: AuditTest.testTheOnlyFlagStillHolds... exit <n>
- the audit's page filter removed: exit <n>
- the quiz gate's page filter removed: exit <n>
- an orphan key reported under any --only: QuizzesTest.testTheOnlyFlagReports... exit <n>
- the fork gate's page filter removed: ForkGateTest.testTheOnlyFlagGives... exit <n>
- the gate-times line removed: AuditTest.testCheckEndsWithEachGatesTime exit <n>
- --only accepted without --check: AuditTest.testTheOnlyFlagNeedsCheck... exit <n>
- the proof gate's page filter removed: ProofGateTest.testTheOnlyFlagHolds... exit <n>
BuildTest <n> tests, 0 failed.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
```

  `git log -1 --format=%B | tail -1` prints the Co-Authored-By line and nothing follows it. Delete
  build/.work/commit-msg.txt afterwards.

### Task 9: Acceptance: every page of the real course, as a reader uses it, in three browsers

**Why.** The gates prove the course's files (stated outputs, the differential gate, the vocabulary). Nothing yet proves
the course as a reader meets it: a real page, at a phone's width, in Safari's, Chrome's and Firefox's engines, driven
only through its own buttons. The author asked for the first validation of each chapter to be automated in headless
browsers, so that only chapter 1 and chapters whose acceptance is less certain reach the author as a page to review
(Task 10). This task is that automation.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/test/acceptance.mjs`
- Create: `web/test/vol-accept/volume.json`
- Create: `web/test/vol-accept/content/ch01-first-steps.md`
- Create: `web/test/vol-accept/content/_boxes.json`
- Create: `web/test/vol-accept/content/_checks.json`
- Create: `web/test/vol-accept/content/_solutions.md`
- Create: `web/test/vol-accept/practice/ch01-first-steps-practice.md`
- Modify: `ci/lib.sh:107-113` (`browser_steps`: two steps)
- Modify: `ci/test.mjs:16-38` (header, one bullet), `:79` (`root` copies `weekly.sh` too), after `:161` (one new case),
  `:335` (the expected count, 28 to 29)
- Modify: `README.md:168` (one line in the test list) and after `:182` (one paragraph)
- Test: `web/test/acceptance.mjs` is the test (of the course, and, with `--volume web/test/vol-accept`, of itself);
  `ci/test.mjs` holds the weekly wiring.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes:
  - `web/test/harness.mjs`: `REPO`, `ENGINES`, `check(label, ok, got) -> ok`, `done()`, `buildSite(volumeDir, name) -> project dir`,
    `open(engine, dir, pagePath, contextOptions) -> { page, offsite, errors, close }`.
  - `web/page/transcript.js`: `tidy(s)`, `statedStderr(stderr)`; `web/page/support.js`: `FATAL`.
  - The built page as `build/Pages.java` and `web/page/box.js` make it: `.box[data-box][data-kind]` (`run`, `input`,
    `raises`, `compile-error`, `predict`, `reference`), `data-check` (a predict box's answer in its `expected`),
    `textarea.code`, `textarea.prediction`, buttons `Run`, `Check`, `Reset`, `End input`, `input.answer`, `.box[data-state]`
    (`done` or `waiting` when settled), `.run`'s children (text, `span.typed`, `span.err`, `span.err.javac`, `p.status`),
    `.verdict[data-done=yes] p.summary`.
  - `site/boxes.json`: per box `{ id, page, source, expected, reference, raises, compileError, varies, stdin, why, check }`.
  - Interface 1 (`volumes/vol1/`), interface 2 (`_solutions.md` with `### mistake`), interface 3 (`noCheck`), and the
    reveal as the reveal task builds it (`.box > details.solution`, its `summary` "Show a solution" and its `pre`).
- Produces:
  - `node web/test/acceptance.mjs [--volume <dir>] <chNN|all> [chromium|webkit|firefox]`: exit 0 when every check
    passed, 1 when one failed (or a volume has no chapter), 2 on misuse or a missing volume or chapter. With no volume
    under `volumes/` at all it prints `no volumes` and exits 0, as `Build.java --check` does.
  - Per chapter and engine, `build/.work/acceptance/<chNN>/<engine>/` (for another volume,
    `build/.work/acceptance-<volume slug>/<chNN>/<engine>/`), emptied at the start of each run:
    - `<page>.png`: the page, full length, at 390 px, after every box has run.
    - `boxes/<page>-<n>.png`: box `<page>#<n>` after its Run.
    - `results.json`:
      `{ volume, chapter, engine, platform, seconds, checks, failed, pages: [ { page, seconds, shot, boxes: [ { id, kind,
      check (kind or null), noCheck (words or null), varies, starter, proof: [ { label, answer, want ("pass"|"fail"),
      ms, summary, ok } ], reveal ({ solution, ok } or null), run ({ shown, want?, status: [..], ms, ok } or null),
      skipped (words or null), shot } ] } ] }`. Task 10 reads it.
  - The fixture volume `web/test/vol-accept/` (slug `vol-accept`): one box of every kind the acceptance drives.
  - Two steps in every browser list (`ci/lib.sh browser_steps`), so `bash ci/weekly.sh <engine>` (CI, weekly) and
    `bash ci/everything.sh` (the Mac) run them: `web-acceptance-fixture-<engine>` and `web-acceptance-<engine>`.

**How long it takes, and the command lines.** Estimates from what the existing tests take, to be replaced by the
first measured run (the script prints each page's seconds and each chapter's). `page.mjs` took 192 s for all three
engines when it was written (Plan 3b), about a minute an engine, of which about 40 s is spent waiting out its
deliberate 5 s deadlines and absences; DESIGN section 1 gives 0.54 s for a cold Hello World and 0.24 s warm in
Chromium. Every acceptance action is one compile and one run (an input check's Check, one compile and a run per
declared run). Chapter 1, adapted from the Python chapter (10 boxes, a predict check, 3 worked solutions; a practice
page of 7 output-checked steps), is about 20 Runs and 25 Checks: about 45 compile-and-runs at 0.3 to 0.6 s, two loads
of each page, about 20 box screenshots and 2 full-page ones, and one plain build of the site per run of the script
(shared by every chapter that run accepts; about 10 s). A later chapter (about 13 boxes, 8 practice steps, 12 checked
boxes at 3 or 4 answers each) is about 65 compile-and-runs. Firefox is taken as twice Chromium's time, as the jshell
check measured in a browser (118 s, 112 s and 261 s); that ratio is the least certain number here.

| Run | Chromium | WebKit | Firefox |
|---|---|---|---|
| the fixture, `vol-accept` (Mac) | about 30 s | about 30 s | about 60 s |
| chapter 1 (Mac) | about 1 min | about 1 min | about 2 min |
| a later chapter (Mac) | 1 to 1.5 min | 1 to 1.5 min | 2 to 3 min |
| all twelve chapters (Mac) | about 15 min | about 15 min | about 30 min |
| all twelve on GitHub's runner | about 35 min | about 45 min | about 90 min |

The runner row uses the weekly browser jobs' own ratio of runner to Mac: Chromium 1192 s against 476 s (2.5 times),
WebKit 1528 against 514, Firefox 3460 against 1141 (3 times). Watch item: the Firefox weekly job takes about 58
minutes today, so the whole course's acceptance takes it past its 120-minute limit before chapter 12. After chapter 1,
compare the logged seconds with this table; when the Firefox job's projected time passes 100 minutes, the acceptance
moves into a weekly job of its own (a later task, decided with those numbers).

```
node web/test/acceptance.mjs ch01                  # chapter 1 of the course, in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox in turn
node web/test/acceptance.mjs ch01 webkit           # in one engine
node web/test/acceptance.mjs all                   # every chapter
node web/test/acceptance.mjs --volume web/test/vol-accept all   # the acceptance's own fixture
bash -c 'source ci/lib.sh && node web/test/acceptance.mjs ch01' # a clone with no runtime/dist/fork
bash ci/weekly.sh webkit                           # the weekly WebKit list (CI), the acceptance in it
bash ci/everything.sh                              # the Mac's whole suite, the acceptance in each engine's list
```

It runs after the build's own gates (`$J build/Build.java --check`), which it does not repeat.

`````markdown
# Chapter 1: First steps

A program prints:

```java
void main() {
    IO.println("Hello, world!");
}
```

```output
Hello, world!
```

It can ask a question and use the answer:

```java
void main() {
    String name = IO.readln("Your name? ");
    IO.println("Hello, " + name + "!");
}
```

```output
Your name? Ada
Hello, Ada!
```

This one crashes on purpose:

```java
void main() {
    int[] a = new int[3];
    IO.println(a[3]);
}
```

```output
Exception in thread "main" java.lang.ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: Index 3 out of bounds for length 3
	at Main.main(Main.java:3)
```

This one does not compile, on purpose:

```java
void main() {
    IO.println(count);
}
```

### Try It

1. Write `isEven`, which says whether a number is even:

```java
boolean isEven(int n) {
    return n % 2 == 0;
}

void main() {
    IO.println(isEven(4));
}
```

```output
true
```

2. Make it print `Ada`:

```java
void main() {
    IO.println("Hi");
}
```

```output
Hi
```

3. Ask for a number and print it doubled:

```java
void main() {
    int n = Integer.parseInt(IO.readln("Number: "));
    IO.println(n);
}
```

```output
Number: 5
5
```

4. What does this print?

```java
void main() {
    int x = 3;
    IO.println(x + 2);
    IO.println("x" + 2);
}
```

```output
5
x2
```

5. Print a box of stars, any size:

```java
void main() {
    IO.println("*");
}
```

```output
*
```

A syntax reminder is not a program:

```java
if (condition) {
```

A set prints in an order of its own:

```java
void main() {
    IO.println(java.util.Set.of("a", "b", "c", "d", "e", "f"));
}
```

```output
[a, b, c, d, e, f]
```

(3) `web/test/acceptance.mjs`:

```js
/*
 *  Copyright 2026 Groundwork contributors.
 *
 *  Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
 *  you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
 *  You may obtain a copy of the License at
 *
 *       http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
 *
 *  Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
 *  distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
 *  WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
 *  See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
 *  limitations under the License.
 */
// The acceptance: every page of a chapter of the real course, as a reader uses it, in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox at
// 390 px, through the page's own controls and nothing else (no call into the page's code). It builds the volume as the
// course is built (harness buildSite), then on each of the chapter's pages, the chapter and its practice page:
// - Check, three ways (DESIGN section 3): each worked solution in _solutions.md, typed into its box, must pass and each
//   mistake must fail; a predict box takes its stated output and each right prediction, which must pass, and each wrong
//   prediction, which must fail. A box with noCheck says so in its own words and offers no Check.
// - Show a solution: a box with a worked solution offers it, shut as the page loads, and no other box does; opening
//   it shows that box's own first worked solution, and every other box's stays shut.
// - On a fresh load, Run on every box that runs, each question answered in place with the box's declared stdin, as a
//   reader types it: the box must show its stated output; a raises box ends in its exception's line (in WebKit with no
//   "at" lines under it, D42); a compileError box shows javac's error.
// - Nothing scrolls sideways at 390 px, no request leaves the page's origin, and no page error happens.
// It saves a screenshot of each page and of each box, and what it saw (results.json), under
// build/.work/acceptance/<chNN>/<engine>/ (build/.work/acceptance-<slug>/... for another volume), and prints how long
// each page took. It never opens the scratchpad (web/test/scratchpad.mjs holds that, D115), and it runs after the
// build's own gates (Build.java --check), which it does not repeat.
//   node web/test/acceptance.mjs [--volume <dir>] <chNN|all> [chromium|webkit|firefox]
// The course is volumes/vol1; while no volume exists it says "no volumes" and passes, as Build.java --check does. Its
// fixture, web/test/vol-accept, holds a box of every kind it drives.
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { REPO, ENGINES, check, done, buildSite, open } from "./harness.mjs";
import { tidy, statedStderr } from "../page/transcript.js";
import { FATAL } from "../page/support.js";

const USAGE = "usage: node web/test/acceptance.mjs [--volume <dir>] <chNN|all> [chromium|webkit|firefox]";
const COURSE = path.join(REPO, "volumes/vol1");
// What a box shows when its run went wrong in a way no stated output allows (web/page/box.js's STATUS): the fatal
// error, a run worker that died, and a replay that printed something else the second time.
const BROKEN = [FATAL, "The program stopped unexpectedly.", "This program printed something different when it ran again"];
// WebKit prints an uncaught exception's first line only (D42, web/test/runner.mjs's NO_FRAMES): there a stated output
// is compared without the program's own frames, the "at" lines a terminal shows under that line.
const NO_FRAMES = ["webkit"];
const OWN_FRAME = /^\tat [^()]*\(Main\.java:\d+\)$/; // a frame of the program's own, as transcript.js and Audit.java keep it
// Where a program that fills memory takes the whole page down: Chromium (D41), and on Linux WebKit and Firefox too
// (D113), as web/test/page.mjs's CRASHES_THE_PAGE records it. A box declared to raise OutOfMemoryError is not run
// there; the skip is printed and kept in results.json, never passed in silence.
const LOSES_THE_PAGE = ["chromium", ...(process.platform === "linux" ? ["webkit", "firefox"] : [])];
// A verdict that says the answer is right (web/page/box.js): a method or input check's count, an output or predict
// check's "Correct.".
const PASSED = /^(Passed all \d+ (cases|runs)\.|Correct\.)$/;
// The reveal (build/Pages.java's appendBox): a box with a worked solution has, as a direct child, a native
// <details class="solution"> whose summary says these words and whose <pre> holds the first worked solution.
const SHOW = "Show a solution";
const SOLUTION = (id) => `.box[data-box="${id}"] > details.solution`;
// What each box offers as the page loads: its direct details.solution children, the summary's words, and whether any is
// open. Runs in the page.
const REVEALS_AT_LOAD = () => Object.fromEntries([...document.querySelectorAll(".box")].map((b) => {
  const d = [...b.children].filter((c) => c.matches("details.solution"));
  return [b.dataset.box, { count: d.length, summary: d[0]?.querySelector(":scope > summary")?.textContent ?? null, open: d.some((x) => x.open) }];
}));
// Bounds on a wait, so a box that never settles fails instead of hanging; the page's own limit is 5 s a run.
const RUN_MS = 30_000, CHECK_MS = 60_000;
// A phone's 390 px (Firefox has no isMobile: a desktop window 390 px wide), as web/test/scratchpad.mjs opens it.
const PHONE = (engine) => ({ viewport: { width: 390, height: 844 }, ...(engine === "firefox" ? {} : { isMobile: true }) });

const args = process.argv.slice(2);
let volumeDir = COURSE;
if (args[0] === "--volume") {
  if (!args[1]) { console.log(USAGE); process.exit(2); }
  volumeDir = path.resolve(REPO, args[1]);
  args.splice(0, 2);
}
const [which, only] = args;
if (args.length < 1 || args.length > 2 || !/^(ch\d+|all)$/.test(which) || (only && !ENGINES[only])) { console.log(USAGE); process.exit(2); }
if (!fs.existsSync(path.join(volumeDir, "volume.json"))) {
  const volumes = path.join(REPO, "volumes");
  const any = fs.readdirSync(volumes).some((d) => fs.existsSync(path.join(volumes, d, "volume.json")));
  // As Build.java --check: with no volume at all there is nothing to accept, which is no failure. Once a volume
  // exists, a course that is missing is one.
  if (volumeDir === COURSE && !any) { console.log("no volumes"); process.exit(0); }
  console.log(`no volume at ${path.relative(REPO, volumeDir)} (no volume.json)`);
  process.exit(2);
}
const slug = path.basename(volumeDir);
const mdIn = (d) => (fs.existsSync(d) ? fs.readdirSync(d).filter((f) => f.endsWith(".md")).sort() : []);
const pageFiles = [...mdIn(path.join(volumeDir, "content")), ...mdIn(path.join(volumeDir, "practice"))];
const chapters = [...new Set(pageFiles.map((f) => /^(ch\d+)-/.exec(f)?.[1]).filter(Boolean))].sort();
if (!chapters.length) { console.log(`${path.relative(REPO, volumeDir)} has no chapter: nothing was accepted`); process.exit(1); }
if (which !== "all" && !chapters.includes(which)) {
  console.log(`no chapter ${which} in ${path.relative(REPO, volumeDir)}; it has ${chapters.join(", ")}`);
  process.exit(2);
}
// The chapter's page, then its practice page: the slugs the build publishes them under.
const pagesOf = (ch) => pageFiles.filter((f) => f.startsWith(`${ch}-`)).map((f) => f.slice(0, -3));
const json = (f) => (fs.existsSync(f) ? JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(f, "utf8")) : {});
const DECL = json(path.join(volumeDir, "content/_boxes.json"));
const CHECKS = json(path.join(volumeDir, "content/_checks.json"));
const SOLUTIONS = readSolutions(path.join(volumeDir, "content/_solutions.md"));
const NONE = { rightJava: [], rightText: [], mistakeJava: [], mistakeText: [] };
const site = path.join(buildSite(volumeDir, `acceptance-site-${slug}`), "site");
const BOXES = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(site, "boxes.json"), "utf8"));
const OUT = path.join(REPO, "build/.work", slug === "vol1" ? "acceptance" : `acceptance-${slug}`);

const boxOf = (t, id) => t.page.locator(`.box[data-box="${id}"]`);
const stateOf = (t, id) => t.page.evaluate((id) => document.querySelector(`.box[data-box="${id}"]`).dataset.state, id);
const settled = (t, id) => t.page.waitForFunction((id) =>
  ["done", "waiting"].includes(document.querySelector(`.box[data-box="${id}"]`).dataset.state), id, { timeout: RUN_MS });
const ready = (t) => t.page.waitForFunction(() => document.documentElement.dataset.java, null, { timeout: RUN_MS });
const withoutFrames = (s) => s.split("\n").filter((l) => !OWN_FRAME.test(l)).join("\n");

// What a box's .run shows, taken apart as web/page/box.js puts it together: the program's output with the answers
// typed in place (text nodes and span.typed), its stderr (span.err), javac's error (span.javac) and the box's status
// lines (p.status). Runs in the page.
const READ_RUN = (run) => {
  const r = { out: "", err: "", javac: "", status: [] };
  for (const n of run.childNodes) {
    if (n.nodeType === Node.TEXT_NODE || n.matches("span.typed")) r.out += n.textContent;
    else if (n.matches("span.javac")) r.javac += n.textContent;
    else if (n.matches("span.err")) r.err += n.textContent;
    else if (n.matches("p.status")) r.status.push(n.textContent);
  }
  return r;
};
// What runs wider than a phone at 390 px: the page, a box (which hides its overflow, so the page's own width cannot
// see it), or a verdict's or a case list's own text. Code, outputs and javac's errors scroll inside their own box on
// purpose (web/test/page.mjs), so a pre or textarea, and anything inline, is not measured. A desktop Firefox window
// has no phone viewport to compare (no isMobile). Runs in the page.
const OVERFLOW = (firefox) => {
  const wide = [];
  if (document.documentElement.scrollWidth > 390) wide.push(`the page ${document.documentElement.scrollWidth}`);
  if (!firefox && visualViewport.width !== 390) wide.push(`laid out ${visualViewport.width} wide`);
  for (const el of document.querySelectorAll(".box, .box .verdict, .box .verdict *, .box .cases, .box .cases *")) {
    if (el.closest("pre, textarea") || getComputedStyle(el).display === "inline") continue;
    if (el.scrollWidth > el.clientWidth)
      wide.push(`${el.closest(".box").dataset.box}: ${el.tagName.toLowerCase()}.${el.className} ${el.scrollWidth} > ${el.clientWidth}`);
  }
  return wide;
};

// _solutions.md as Plan 5 extends it, read as build/Markdown.java reads fences and headings: under "## <box id>", the
// java fences before any level-3 heading are worked solutions (the first is the one a reader may reveal) and the text
// fences there are further right predictions; under "### mistake", java fences are mistakes and text fences wrong
// predictions. A fence opens on a line of three backticks and a language and closes on a line of three backticks; its
// body is each line followed by "\n".
function readSolutions(file) {
  const sections = new Map();
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return sections;
  const lines = fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").replace(/\r\n?/g, "\n").split("\n");
  let id = null, part = null;
  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
    const fence = /^```([^`]*)$/.exec(lines[i]);
    if (fence) {
      let body = "";
      for (i++; i < lines.length && lines[i] !== "```"; i++) body += lines[i] + "\n";
      const lang = fence[1].trim(), s = id == null ? null : sections.get(id);
      if (s && part && (lang === "java" || lang === "text")) s[part + (lang === "java" ? "Java" : "Text")].push(body);
      continue;
    }
    const h = /^(#{1,3}) (.*)$/.exec(lines[i]);
    if (!h) continue;
    if (h[1].length === 2) {
      id = h[2].trim();
      part = "right";
      if (!sections.has(id)) sections.set(id, { rightJava: [], rightText: [], mistakeJava: [], mistakeText: [] });
    } else if (h[1].length === 3) part = h[2].trim() === "mistake" ? "mistake" : null;
    else id = part = null;
  }
  return sections;
}

// Check, three ways, through the box's own field and button; then the box is put back as it was built.
async function prove(t, e, r, expect, wide, engine) {
  const box = boxOf(t, e.id), s = SOLUTIONS.get(e.id) ?? NONE, predict = r.kind === "predict";
  const answers = predict
    ? [{ label: "the stated output", answer: JSON.parse(await box.getAttribute("data-check")).expected, want: "pass" },
      ...s.rightText.map((a, i) => ({ label: `right prediction ${i + 2}`, answer: a, want: "pass" })),
      ...s.mistakeText.map((a, i) => ({ label: `wrong prediction ${i + 1}`, answer: a, want: "fail" }))]
    : [...s.rightJava.map((a, i) => ({ label: `worked solution ${i + 1}`, answer: a, want: "pass" })),
      ...s.mistakeJava.map((a, i) => ({ label: `mistake ${i + 1}`, answer: a, want: "fail" }))];
  const rights = answers.filter((a) => a.want === "pass").length, wrongs = answers.length - rights;
  expect(`${e.id}: its ${e.check.kind} check has ${predict ? "a wrong prediction" : "two worked solutions and a mistake"} to prove it with`,
    (predict || rights >= 2) && wrongs >= 1, { rights, wrongs });
  const field = box.locator(predict ? "textarea.prediction" : "textarea.code");
  for (const a of answers) {
    await field.fill(a.answer);
    const t0 = Date.now();
    await box.getByRole("button", { name: "Check", exact: true }).click();
    await t.page.waitForFunction((id) => document.querySelector(`.box[data-box="${id}"] .verdict`)?.dataset.done === "yes",
      e.id, { timeout: CHECK_MS });
    a.ms = Date.now() - t0;
    a.summary = (await box.locator(".verdict p.summary").innerText()).replace(/\r/g, "").trim();
    a.ok = a.summary !== FATAL && PASSED.test(a.summary) === (a.want === "pass");
    expect(`${e.id}: ${a.label} ${a.want === "pass" ? "passes" : "fails"} its Check`, a.ok, { summary: a.summary, answer: a.answer });
    r.proof.push(a);
    wide.push(...await t.page.evaluate(OVERFLOW, engine === "firefox"));
  }
  if (predict) await field.fill("");
  else {
    const reset = box.getByRole("button", { name: "Reset", exact: true });
    if (await reset.isVisible()) await reset.click();
  }
}

// What each box offers to reveal as the page loads (acceptPage checks it before anything is pressed): a box with a
// worked solution offers one Show a solution, shut; every other box offers none.
function revealsAtLoad(entries, atLoad, expect) {
  for (const e of entries) {
    const a = atLoad[e.id] ?? { count: 0, summary: null, open: false };
    if (SOLUTIONS.get(e.id)?.rightJava[0] === undefined) expect(`${e.id}: has no worked solution, and offers none`, a.count === 0, a);
    else expect(`${e.id}: offers its worked solution behind "${SHOW}", shut as the page loads`,
      a.count === 1 && a.summary === SHOW && !a.open, a);
  }
}

// Show a solution, pressed as a reader presses it: its summary opens that box's own details, whose pre is the box's
// first worked solution, and every other box's stays shut. Then it is shut again by its summary, so the next box starts
// from the page as it loaded.
async function reveal(t, e, r, expect, wide, engine) {
  const first = SOLUTIONS.get(e.id)?.rightJava[0];
  if (first === undefined || await t.page.locator(SOLUTION(e.id)).count() !== 1) return; // revealsAtLoad judged it
  await t.page.locator(`${SOLUTION(e.id)} > summary`).click();
  await t.page.waitForTimeout(300); // an absence: time for a page script answering the toggle to open another box's
  const seen = await t.page.locator(SOLUTION(e.id)).evaluate((d) => ({ open: d.open,
    text: d.querySelector(":scope > pre")?.textContent ?? null,
    others: [...document.querySelectorAll(".box > details.solution")].filter((x) => x !== d && x.open).map((x) => x.parentElement.dataset.box) }));
  seen.visible = await t.page.locator(`${SOLUTION(e.id)} > pre`).isVisible();
  const ok = seen.open && seen.visible && seen.text != null && tidy(seen.text) === tidy(first) && seen.others.length === 0;
  r.reveal = { solution: first, ok };
  expect(`${e.id}: "${SHOW}" opens that box's own first worked solution, and every other box's stays shut`, ok, seen);
  wide.push(...await t.page.evaluate(OVERFLOW, engine === "firefox"));
  if (seen.open) await t.page.locator(`${SOLUTION(e.id)} > summary`).click();
}

// Run, as a reader runs the box: each question answered in place with the next declared answer, and End input past
// the last one, as the build's stdin ends there (web/test/replay.mjs).
async function runBox(t, e, r, expect, engine) {
  const box = boxOf(t, e.id);
  if (e.raises === "java.lang.OutOfMemoryError" && LOSES_THE_PAGE.includes(engine)) {
    r.skipped = `not run: in ${engine} on ${process.platform} a program that fills memory takes the page down (D41, D113)`;
    console.log(`  skip  ${engine}: ${e.id}: ${r.skipped}`);
    return;
  }
  const t0 = Date.now();
  await box.getByRole("button", { name: "Run", exact: true }).click();
  await settled(t, e.id);
  const typed = [...e.stdin];
  for (let i = 0; i <= e.stdin.length && await stateOf(t, e.id) === "waiting"; i++) {
    if (typed.length) {
      await box.locator("input.answer").fill(typed.shift());
      await box.locator("input.answer").press("Enter");
    } else await box.getByRole("button", { name: "End input", exact: true }).click();
    await settled(t, e.id);
  }
  const ms = Date.now() - t0;
  const got = await box.locator(".run").evaluate(READ_RUN);
  const waiting = await stateOf(t, e.id) === "waiting";
  const shown = r.kind === "compile-error" ? got.javac : got.out + statedStderr(got.err);
  r.run = { shown, status: got.status, ms, ok: false };
  let ok = !waiting && typed.length === 0 && !got.status.some((s) => BROKEN.some((b) => s.startsWith(b))), label;
  if (r.kind === "compile-error") {
    const first = got.javac.split("\n")[0];
    ok = ok && /^Main\.java:\d+: error: /.test(first) && first.includes(e.compileError);
    label = `Run shows javac's error: ${e.compileError}`;
  } else {
    const line = got.err.split("\n")[0], thrown = `Exception in thread "main" ${e.raises}`;
    if (e.raises) ok = ok && (line === thrown || line.startsWith(`${thrown}: `));
    else if (e.expected != null || e.varies) ok = ok && !/^Exception in thread/m.test(got.err);
    if (e.expected != null && !e.varies) {
      const want = NO_FRAMES.includes(engine) ? withoutFrames(e.expected) : e.expected;
      r.run.want = want;
      ok = ok && got.status.length === 0 && tidy(shown) === tidy(want);
      label = `Run shows its stated output${e.stdin.length ? `, its ${e.stdin.length} answer(s) typed in place` : ""}` +
        (e.raises ? `, ending in ${e.raises}${NO_FRAMES.includes(engine) ? " without its at lines (D42)" : ""}` : "");
    } else label = e.varies ? "Run runs to its end (varies: its output is compared with nothing)"
      : `Run runs${r.starter ? " its starter" : ""}, and the box shows nothing broken`;
  }
  r.run.ok = ok;
  expect(`${e.id}: ${label}`, ok, { shown, status: got.status, waiting, unused: typed });
}

async function acceptPage(engine, pageSlug, dir, report) {
  const expect = (label, ok, got) => {
    report.checks++;
    if (!check(`${engine}: ${pageSlug}: ${label}`, ok, got)) report.failed++;
    return ok;
  };
  const t0 = Date.now();
  const entries = BOXES.filter((b) => b.page === pageSlug);
  const result = { page: pageSlug, seconds: null, shot: `${pageSlug}.png`, boxes: [] };
  const wide = [];
  const t = await open(engine, site, `${slug}/${pageSlug}.html`, PHONE(engine));
  try {
    await ready(t);
    expect("the page can run Java", await t.page.evaluate(() => document.documentElement.dataset.java) === "ready", null);
    const onPage = await t.page.locator(".box").count();
    expect(`it shows the ${entries.length} boxes the build published, and each is driven below`,
      entries.length > 0 && onPage === entries.length, { onPage, published: entries.length });
    revealsAtLoad(entries, await t.page.evaluate(REVEALS_AT_LOAD), expect);
    for (const e of entries) {
      const r = { id: e.id, kind: null, check: e.check?.kind ?? null, noCheck: DECL[e.id]?.noCheck ?? null, varies: e.varies,
        starter: CHECKS[e.id]?.starter != null, proof: [], reveal: null, run: null, skipped: null, shot: null };
      result.boxes.push(r);
      try {
        r.kind = await boxOf(t, e.id).getAttribute("data-kind");
        const checkButtons = () => boxOf(t, e.id).getByRole("button", { name: "Check", exact: true }).count();
        if (e.check) await prove(t, e, r, expect, wide, engine);
        else if (r.noCheck != null) {
          const text = await boxOf(t, e.id).innerText();
          expect(`${e.id}: says why it has no Check, in its noCheck words, and offers no Check`,
            text.includes(r.noCheck) && await checkButtons() === 0, text.slice(0, 300));
        } else if (r.kind !== "reference") expect(`${e.id}: has no check, and offers no Check`, await checkButtons() === 0, null);
        await reveal(t, e, r, expect, wide, engine);
      } catch (err) { expect(`${e.id}: could be checked to the end`, false, String(err).slice(0, 300)); }
    }
    // A fresh load, as a reader comes to the page: every box runs, and stays run for the screenshots.
    await t.page.reload();
    await ready(t);
    for (const r of result.boxes) {
      const e = entries.find((x) => x.id === r.id);
      try {
        if (r.kind !== "reference" && r.kind !== "predict") await runBox(t, e, r, expect, engine);
        r.shot = `boxes/${pageSlug}-${r.id.slice(r.id.indexOf("#") + 1)}.png`;
        await boxOf(t, r.id).screenshot({ path: path.join(dir, r.shot) });
      } catch (err) { expect(`${r.id}: could be run to the end`, false, String(err).slice(0, 300)); }
    }
    wide.push(...await t.page.evaluate(OVERFLOW, engine === "firefox"));
    await t.page.screenshot({ path: path.join(dir, result.shot), fullPage: true });
  } catch (err) { expect("the page could be driven to the end", false, String(err).slice(0, 300)); }
  expect("nothing scrolls sideways at 390 px, with every verdict, revealed solution and output shown", wide.length === 0, [...new Set(wide)]);
  expect("no request left the page's origin", t.offsite.length === 0, t.offsite);
  expect("no page error", t.errors.length === 0, t.errors);
  await t.close();
  result.seconds = Math.round((Date.now() - t0) / 1000);
  console.log(`  ${engine}: ${pageSlug} took ${result.seconds} s`);
  return result;
}

// The fixture holds a box of every kind this test drives; a fixture that lost one fails here rather than passing
// having driven less (as web/test/replay.mjs holds its fixture's input boxes).
function covered(engine, report) {
  const seen = new Set();
  for (const b of report.pages.flatMap((p) => p.boxes)) {
    seen.add(b.kind);
    if (b.check) seen.add(`check:${b.check}`);
    if (b.varies) seen.add("varies");
    if (b.noCheck != null) seen.add("noCheck");
    if (b.starter) seen.add("starter");
    if (b.reveal) seen.add("reveal");
  }
  const want = ["run", "input", "raises", "compile-error", "predict", "reference", "varies", "check:method", "check:output",
    "check:input", "check:predict", "noCheck", "starter", "reveal"];
  const missing = want.filter((k) => !seen.has(k));
  report.checks++;
  if (!check(`${engine}: the fixture drove a box of every kind (${want.join(", ")})`, missing.length === 0, missing)) report.failed++;
}

for (const engine of only ? [only] : Object.keys(ENGINES)) {
  console.log(engine);
  for (const ch of which === "all" ? chapters : [which]) {
    const dir = path.join(OUT, ch, engine);
    fs.rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
    fs.mkdirSync(path.join(dir, "boxes"), { recursive: true });
    const report = { volume: slug, chapter: ch, engine, platform: process.platform, seconds: null, checks: 0, failed: 0, pages: [] };
    const t0 = Date.now();
    for (const p of pagesOf(ch)) report.pages.push(await acceptPage(engine, p, dir, report));
    if (slug === "vol-accept") covered(engine, report);
    report.seconds = Math.round((Date.now() - t0) / 1000);
    fs.writeFileSync(path.join(dir, "results.json"), JSON.stringify(report, null, 1) + "\n");
    console.log(`  ${engine}: ${ch}: ${report.checks} check(s), ${report.failed} failed, ${report.seconds} s`);
  }
}
done();
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`ci/lib.sh`, `browser_steps` (lines 107-113) becomes:

```bash
# weekly <engine>
browser_steps() { # browser_steps <engine>
  local e=$1 t
  for t in runner page scratchpad-panel scratchpad doors zip; do step "web-$t-$e" node "web/test/$t.mjs" "$e"; done
  # The acceptance: first on its own fixture, which proves it, then on the course, every chapter as a reader uses it.
  step "web-acceptance-fixture-$e" node web/test/acceptance.mjs --volume web/test/vol-accept all "$e"
  step "web-acceptance-$e" node web/test/acceptance.mjs all "$e"
  step "jshell-check-$e" node runtime/jshell/test/check.mjs --browser "$e"
  user_prefs_step "jshell-check-$e-no-user-prefs"
}
```

That puts both in `bash ci/weekly.sh <engine>` (weekly.yml's browser matrix, inside its 6 GiB scope) and, through
`ci/everything.sh`'s loop over the three engines, in the Mac's whole suite. Nothing changes in `weekly.yml`.

`README.md`, after line 168 (`node web/test/zip.mjs [engine] …`), add:

```
node web/test/acceptance.mjs <chNN|all> [engine]   # every page of a chapter of the course, as a reader uses it
```

and after line 182 (`` `smoke.mjs` and the like). ``), add a blank line and:

```
`acceptance.mjs` accepts the course as a reader meets it. It builds `volumes/vol1` into
`build/.work/acceptance-site-vol1`, then, for a chapter (`ch01`, or `all`), drives each of its pages at 390 px through
the page's own buttons: every checked box passes each worked solution in `_solutions.md` and fails each mistake, Show a
solution shows that box's worked solution, and every box runs, its questions answered in place, and shows its stated
output. Screenshots and what it saw (`results.json`) go to `build/.work/acceptance/<chNN>/<engine>/`. With
`--volume web/test/vol-accept` it runs on its own fixture; with no volume yet it says `no volumes` and passes.
```

The wider suites, which must stay green: `$J build/BuildTest.java` (ends `<N> test(s), 0 failed`; NameTest and
DecisionsTest read the new files) and `node web/test/page.mjs chromium` (ends `… 0 failed`).

- [ ] **Step 5: Prove the gate by breaking it on purpose**

Save the files the breaks touch, then make each break alone, run, restore, and check the restore:

```
rm -rf build/.work/acceptance-breaks && mkdir -p build/.work/acceptance-breaks
cp -R web/test/vol-accept build/.work/acceptance-breaks/vol-accept
cp web/app.js web/app.css build/.work/acceptance-breaks/
cp build/Pages.java build/.work/acceptance-breaks/Pages.java
NOCHECK=$(git grep -l 'noCheck' -- build/Pages.java web/page | head -1); echo "$NOCHECK"; cp "$NOCHECK" build/.work/acceptance-breaks/nocheck.src
F=web/test/vol-accept/content
A="node web/test/acceptance.mjs --volume web/test/vol-accept ch01 chromium"
```

Each break runs `$A` (Chromium alone) unless it says otherwise; each must end with the named check `FAIL`, the count
line shown, and exit 1. After each, restore with the `cp` back from `build/.work/acceptance-breaks/` and `cmp` the
restored file against its saved copy (silent means identical).

| # | Break (exact edit) | Expected |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | A stated output wrong: `perl -pi -e 's/^Hello, world!$/Hello, world/' $F/ch01-first-steps.md` | `FAIL  chromium: ch01-first-steps: ch01-first-steps#1: Run shows its stated output`; `66 check(s), 1 failed`; exit 1 |
| 2 | The declared answer differs from the transcript: `perl -pi -e 's/"stdin": \["Ada"\]/"stdin": ["Bo"]/' $F/_boxes.json` | `FAIL  … ch01-first-steps#2: Run shows its stated output, its 1 answer(s) typed in place`; `66 check(s), 1 failed`; exit 1 |
| 3 | The frame's line wrong: `perl -pi -e 's/\(Main\.java:3\)$/(Main.java:4)/' $F/ch01-first-steps.md`; run `$A`, then `node web/test/acceptance.mjs --volume web/test/vol-accept ch01 webkit` | Chromium: `FAIL  … ch01-first-steps#3: Run shows its stated output, ending in java.lang.ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException`; `66 check(s), 1 failed`; exit 1.
